{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is classical as well.
We now obtain the result from Proposition
2.5.3.1
.

∎

2.6.6.

Let
C=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x,y]/xy)
as in §
2.6.3
.
Consider the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
C
of horizontal
homothety. I.e., for the corresponding grading on

k[x,y]/xy
,
\deg(x)=1
and
\deg(y)=0
.

Fix a coordinate
t
on the formal disc.
We have a corresponding
map
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
defined as follows.

First, we we have a natural map

{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
This map takes
({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)\in{\mathcal{Z}}
to
s|_{0}\in{\mathcal{L}}|_{0}

for
0\in{\mathcal{D}}
the base-point.

Next, we have a map:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Pol}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt\simeq\prod_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\frac{dt}{t^{i}}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\frac{dt}{t^{n}}={\mathbb{A}}^{1}.

This map records the leading (i.e.,
degree
-n
) coefficient of the connection.

The corresponding map

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
evidently
maps into
C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Remark 2.6.6.1
.

The above is somewhat non-canonical as the second
map above depends
on the choice of coordinate
t
.
The more canonical statement would be
to replace the
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
by the (scheme corresponding
to the) line
t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/t^{-n+1}k[[t]]dt
.

The following result plays a key technical role in our work.

Proposition 2.6.6.2
.

Suppose
n>0
.

(1)

The map
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is flat
along the axes.
34
34

34

By this, we mean that the
corresponding map
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}\operatorname{Spec}(k)\to C
is flat along the axes in the
sense of §
2.6.3
.

(2)

We have a canonical isomorphism:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\simeq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}.

(3)

Let
\iota:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

denote the map:

({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)\mapsto({\mathcal{L}}(1),\nabla,s)

where
{\mathcal{L}}(1)
is the line bundle on the disc
whose sections on the disc are allowed
to have a pole of order
1
at the base-point
0\in{\mathcal{D}}
.
35
35

35

Another way to say this:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
acts on
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
, and the corresponding
action of
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}=\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}(k)
preserves

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
for all
n
; the induced map

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is our
\iota
.

Then
\iota
fits into a (derived) Cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{id},0)}
\textstyle{C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.}

Proof.

For (
1
), it suffices to check
flatness along axes after passing to the fpqc cover

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.
The corresponding map to
C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
evidently lifts
to
C
, and it suffices to check that the corresponding
map to
C
is flat along axes. Using coordinates
as in the proof of
Proposition
2.6.5.1
, we deduce the claim
from Corollary
2.6.4.1
.

The other assertions are immediate from the constructions.
For instance, the diagram in
(
3
) is obviously
classically

Cartesian, so derived Cartesian by (
1
).

∎

Corollary 2.6.6.3
.

The above morphism

\iota:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is an
almost finitely presented
36
36

36

See

[
Lur3
]
Definition 7.2.4.26
for the definition in the affine case.
In the following remark in
loc. cit
., it is shown
that this notion is preserved under base-change.
By
[
Lur4
]
Proposition 4.1.4.3, this condition
can be checked flat locally. Therefore, there
is an evident notion of a representable
morphism of prestacks being locally almost of finite
presentation, and we are using the term in this
sense.
We emphasize that in this setting,
passing to an affine cover of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
,
the corresponding map of classical affine
schemes is a finitely presented morphism in the sense
of classical algebraic geometry,
but being almost finitely presented is a

stronger
notion (in spite of the terminology):
in the more classical reference
[
BGI+
]

Exposé III Définition 1.2, this property
of a morphism is called
pseudo-coherence
.

closed embedding.

Proof.

Almost finite presentation is preserved under
(derived) base-change, and any morphism of
schemes almost of finite presentation
is itself almost of finite presentation.
So the claim follows from
Proposition
2.6.6.2
(
3
).

∎

Corollary 2.6.6.4
.

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
is a classical ind-algebraic stack.
More precisely, the total space of the canonical
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-
torsor on

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
is classical and a reasonable indscheme
in the sense of
[
Ras7
]
§6.8.

Proof.

We clearly have:

(2.6.1)

\operatorname{colim}\big({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\iota}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\iota}\ldots\big)={\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}.

Each of these morphisms is an
almost finitely presented closed
embedding.
Therefore,
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\operatorname{Spec}(k)

is a filtered colimit of the classical affine schemes

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\operatorname{Spec}(k)
under
almost finitely presented closed
embeddings.

We now remind the general definition of reasonable
indscheme from
[
Ras7
]
:
it is a (DG) indscheme that can be written
as a colimit of eventually coconnective
quasi-compact quasi-separated schemes
under closed embeddings almost of finite presentation.
So clearly this property is verified here.

∎

Remark 2.6.6.5
.

One clearly obtains similar results for the Higgs analogue
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
. A posteriori, one sees that

{\mathcal{M}}aps(\mathring{{\mathcal{D}}},C)
is a reasonable ind-affine indscheme.
For a weaker (classical) notion of reasonable indscheme, a similar
result with
C
replaced by any finite type affine scheme
is well-known: see
[
BD
]
Lemma 2.4.8. It is natural to ask: in what
generality is such a result true for the stronger
(derived) notion used here?

Proof of Theorem
2.4.3.1
.

By Corollary
2.6.6.4
,

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}/\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}

is a classical (non-algebraic) prestack.
We deduce the same for
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}
.

∎

2.6.7.

We need one mild improvement of Proposition
2.6.6.2
.
Roughly, the statement says that the use of the base-point
0\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}

is not essential in
loc. cit
.

First, we have a map:

(2.6.2)

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

where the latter map is evaluation. Explicitly, this map
sends
(\tau,({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}
to

s|_{\tau}\in{\mathcal{L}}|_{\tau}
.

Now fix
n
. We let
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]]/t^{n})
.

We have a second map:

(2.6.3)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

defined as follows. By definition, this map will
factor as:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\times\operatorname{Pol}}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

where the last map remains to be defined.
First, note that the dualizing complex

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n})
lies in degree
0

and is a line bundle; it corresponds to the module
(k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}=t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt
. Therefore, given
\tau\in{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
,
we obtain a line
\tau^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})
. Moreover,
a point
\omega\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
defines an evident section
of the above line bundle
\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}
. Restricting to the
point
\tau
, we obtain the desired construction.

Remark 2.6.7.1
.

A choice of coordinate
t
trivializes the above line bundle
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
: the basis element is
\frac{dt}{t^{n}}
.
Such a trivialization lifts the map (
2.6.3
) to
a map to
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. This map is explicitly given at

(\tau,({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))
by evaluating the function

\operatorname{Pol}(\nabla)\cdot\frac{t^{n}}{dt}
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}

at
\tau
.

Proposition 2.6.7.2
.

The map

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
factors
through
C/({\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
. For
n>0
,
the resulting map

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to C/({\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
is flat along
axes.

Proof.

For the first assertion, observe that
for
({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)\in{\mathcal{Z}}
,

s\operatorname{Pol}(\nabla)=0
as a polar section
of the line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}dt
on the disc.
This implies the claim.

For flatness, note that we
have a canonical evaluation morphism:

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}C\to C.

It is immediate from
Proposition
2.6.3.1
that this map
is flat along axes. Indeed, we need to show
that
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}(i_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}}))

is concentrated in cohomological degree
0

(where
i:{\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}\to C
is the embedding).
It suffices to check this after further restriction
to
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}C
, where the assertion is
exactly
loc. cit
.

From here, the claim proceeds as in the proof of
Proposition
2.6.6.2
.

∎

2.7.
Some notation

We now collect a bit of notation related
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

2.7.1.

We begin by introducing notation for various structural maps.

For
n>0
, recall that we have the morphism:

\iota:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}.

For
r\geq 0
, we sometimes let
\iota^{r}
denote the
r
-fold
composition of
\iota
.

We let:

\begin{gathered}\delta_{n}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\\
i_{n}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\\
\pi_{n}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}\\
\lambda_{n}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}\end{gathered}

denote the canonical maps.

We also have the maps
\zeta
and
\widetilde{\zeta}
fitting into
a diagram:

\textstyle{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}_{n}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{n}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}.}

Here the horizontal arrows send

({\mathcal{L}},\nabla)
to
({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,0)
; i.e., we
take
0
as the flat section of our local system.

2.7.2.

We introduce the following line bundle on
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.

Using our base-point
0\in{\mathcal{D}}
, we obtain a canonical line bundle

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(-1)
on
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
; its fiber at

({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)
is the fiber
{\mathcal{L}}|_{0}
of
{\mathcal{L}}
at
0
.

For
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we let:

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(r)\coloneqq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(-1)^{\otimes-r}.

denote its (suitably normalized) tensor powers.
For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
, we let

{\mathcal{F}}(r)
denote its tensor with
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(r)
.

We let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
denote the total space of the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-bundle
defined by
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(-1)
, i.e.,

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}\coloneqq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.
By Proposition
2.5.3.1
(or its proof), we remark that

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
is an affine scheme, and by Theorem
2.4.3.1
,
it is a classical affine scheme. We use notation
\iota
and
\delta_{n}

sometimes for the corresponding maps for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
.

Remark 2.7.2.1
.

Clearly the above line bundle extends canonically to
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
and

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
; we use similar notation in those settings.

2.8.
Fundamental exact sequences

2.8.1.

We now record two exact sequences that we will use for some inductive arguments.
The results here amount to restatements of Proposition
2.6.6.2
.
Suppose
n>0
in what follows.

2.8.2.

We will presently construct a short exact sequence:

(2.8.1)

0\to\delta_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}}(1)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to 0

in
\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}
.

The map
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
is just the
canonical adjunction morphism.

Now observe that there is a canonical map:

(2.8.2)

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(1)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}};

its fiber at
({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)
is the map:

{\mathcal{L}}^{\vee}|_{0}\to{\mathcal{O}}|_{0}

given by pairing with the section
s|_{0}\in{\mathcal{L}}|_{0}
.
We claim that this map factors uniquely as:

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(1)\twoheadrightarrow\delta_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}}(1)\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

and that the resulting map defines a short exact sequence
as in (
2.8.1
).

In fact, this is implicit in work we have done already: these claims are
obtained by pullback from the short exact sequence:

0\to k[x,y]/(xy,x)\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}k[x,y]/xy\to k[x,y]/(xy,y)\to 0

of bi-graded
k[x,y]/xy
modules along the morphism
from Proposition
2.6.6.2
. We emphasize that we are using the
flatness asserted in Proposition
2.6.6.2
(
1
).

Variant 2.8.2.1
.

For later use, we record the following observation. Fix
a coordinate
t
on the disc and use the notation of §
2.10
below.

Then we also have a short exact sequence:

(2.8.3)

0\to\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\delta_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}}\to 0

in
\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}
in which the right
map is the canonical morphism. The left arrow is the unique map fitting
into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{-n}\cdot-}
\textstyle{\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}.}

Here
b_{-n}
is as in §
2.10
below.
Again, the existence of the dotted arrow and the short exact sequence
follow from Proposition
2.6.3.1
.

2.9.
Nice opens

At some points, it is convenient to refer to the following geometric observations
about
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
. Roughly speaking, the idea is that

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is “nice” away from
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}
, which we sometimes use
for inductive statements.

We proceed separately in the cases where
n=1
and
n>1
.

2.9.1.
n>1
case

Define
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\coloneqq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
as the open substack:

{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\coloneqq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\setminus{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}.

Lemma 2.9.1.1
.

The natural map:

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\setminus\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n-1}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{n}

(induced by
\widetilde{\zeta}
from §
2.7.1
)
is an isomorphism.

Remark 2.9.1.2
.

By Proposition
2.3.4.1
, we deduce that
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\simeq({\mathbb{A}}^{1}\setminus 0)\times{\mathbb{A}}^{n-1}\times{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. In particular,

{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
is a smooth stack of finite type.

Proof.

It is convenient to use the notation of §
2.10
, which is introduced
below.
In that notation, we have
{\mathcal{U}}_{n}=\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}])/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

In the (classical) commutative ring
A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}]
, we have relations:

\begin{gathered}0=b_{-n}a_{0}\Rightarrow a_{0}=0\\
0=b_{-n}a_{1}+b_{-n+1}a_{0}=b_{-n}a_{1}\Rightarrow a_{1}=0\\
\ldots\Rightarrow a_{i}=0\text{ for all i}\end{gathered}

(using
n>1
).
It follows that we have an isomorphism:

A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}]=k[b_{-1},\ldots,b_{-n},b_{-n}^{-1}].

This amounts to the claim.

∎

2.9.2.
n=1
case

This case is slightly more technical.

We define
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
as the pro-(Zariski open substack of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}
):

{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\coloneqq\underset{r}{\operatorname{lim}}\mskip 3.0mu{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}\setminus\big({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}\cup\ldots\cup\iota^{r}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0})\big)}.

Here each of the structural maps in the limit is affine,
so the limit exists and is well-behaved.

Lemma 2.9.2.1
.

The natural map:

({\mathbb{A}}^{1}\setminus{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0})\times{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq 1}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\zeta}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}

maps through
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
and induces an isomorphism:

({\mathbb{A}}^{1}\setminus{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0})\times{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{U}}_{1}.

Proof.

We again use the coordinates of §
2.10
.
In this notation,
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
the quotient stack:

\operatorname{Spec}(A_{1}[b_{-1}^{-1},(b_{-1}-1)^{-1},(b_{-1}-2)^{-1},\ldots])/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

obtained by inverting the elements
\{b_{-1}-i\}
for each
i\geq 0
.
In this ring, we have the relations:

(b_{-1}-i)a_{i}=0

for each
i\geq 0
, which means that
a_{i}=0
in this localization
for each
i
. The claim then follows.

∎

2.9.3.

In both cases above, we observe that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{n}\coloneqq\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
is a regular Noetherian
affine scheme.

2.9.4.
A remark

In spite of the above observations, we warn that the natural map:

\zeta:\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\setminus\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq 0}\to{\mathcal{Y}}|_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\setminus\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq 0}}

is
not
an isomorphism. However, it is an isomorphism
if one truncates to irregularity of order
n\leq 1
,
or if one only evaluates on Noetherian
test rings (the left hand side is locally of finite type,
but the right hand side is not).

2.10.
Coordinates

2.10.1.

To make the above completely explicit, we provide explicit
coordinates.

2.10.2.

Define a classical commutative ring
A_{n}
by taking
(infinitely many) generators

a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},\ldots
and
b_{-1},\ldots,b_{-n}

and (infinitely) relations coming from equating Taylor series coefficients
of the formal Laurent series:

\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}ia_{i}t^{i-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{i}t^{i}\cdot\sum_{i=-n}^{-1}b_{-i}t^{-i}.

We consider
A_{n}
as graded with
\deg(a_{i})=1

for all
i
and
\deg(b_{i})=0
.
This grading on
A_{n}
corresponds geometrically
to a
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action on
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})
.

Define a map
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

by sending a point
(a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,b_{-1},\ldots,b_{-n})\in\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})
to the trivial line bundle

{\mathcal{O}}
on the disc and equipping it with the connection

\nabla=d-\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{-i}t^{-i}dt
on the punctured
disc and the section
s=\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}t^{i}
,
which is annihilated by the connection by design.

This map actually factors through
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
; the
latter parametrizes the data of a line
\ell
and
points as above, except that the
a_{i}
are sections
of
\ell
. We then take our line bundle to be

{\mathcal{O}}\otimes\ell
, take
\nabla
given by the same formula,
and similarly for our section
s
.

From Propositions
2.3.4.1
and
2.6.5.1
,
we obtain the next result.

Proposition 2.10.2.1
.

The above map

\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is an isomorphism.
I.e., we have a
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant isomorphism

\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})\simeq\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
.

2.11.
Regular singular sketches

2.11.1.

For the sake of explicitness, we draw pictures of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}
,

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}
, and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq 1}
using
the presentation of §
2.10
.

2.11.2.

By the above,
A_{1}
is the (classical) algebra with generators

b_{-1},a_{0},a_{1},\ldots
and relations:

(b_{-1}-i)a_{i}=0.

For an integer
m\geq 0
, let
A_{1,\leq m}
denote the
subalgebra generated by
b_{-1},a_{0},\ldots,a_{m}
, and let

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq m}^{\leq 1}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}(A_{1,\leq m})
.
We obtain the following picture for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq m}^{\leq 1}
:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq 3}^{\leq 1}=

where the horizontal axis is the
b_{-1}
-axis, and the fiber
over
b_{-1}=i
is given the coordinate
a_{i}
.

The structural maps
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq m+1}^{\leq 1}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq m}^{\leq 1}
coming from the
embedding
A_{1,\leq m}\hookrightarrow A_{1,\leq m+1}
correspond to contracting
the rightmost line in the picture. Therefore, as:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}=\underset{m}{\operatorname{lim}}\mskip 3.0mu\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}_{\leq m}^{\leq 1}

we obtain the picture:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}=
\ldots

where the reddening indicates that interpret the
picture in the
pro
-sense rather than the
ind
-sense.

Remark 2.11.2.1
.

For
n>1
, the stack
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is non-reduced, so there
is additional complexity in attempting to draw pictures.
(In addition, its Krull dimension grows with
n
.)

2.11.3.

The map
\iota:\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}

corresponds to a rightward
shift in the above picture. Therefore,
if we let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}
similarly be the total space
of the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-torsor over
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}
,
from (
2.6.1
) we obtain the picture:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}=
\ldots
\ldots

where the bluing in the picture indicates that we interpret
the limit in the
ind
-sense (and the reddening is as before).

Informally,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}
is a
semi-infinite comb
. It has
infinitely many bristles, which are attached to the handle at integer
points; in the positive direction, these bristles have
pro-nature, and in the negative direction they have ind-nature.
37
37

37

We
are unfortunately unable to produce non-synesthetic
pictures adequately distinguishing between projective and inductive limits.
If asked to better explain the above pictures,
we would resort to the formal descriptions

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}=\operatorname{lim}_{m}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\leq m}^{\leq 1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}=\operatorname{colim}_{\iota}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}
.

2.11.4.

In each of these pictures,
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts by scaling in the vertical direction, i.e.,
scaling the bristles. So forming stacky quotients for this action,
we obtain the promised sketches of

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 1}
.

Finally, the action of
{\mathbb{Z}}=\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq 1}
on
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
is generated
by rightward shift in the above pictures. Quotienting by this action gives a
picture
38
38

38

Roughly speaking, this picture looks like
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{Z}}
with a
single bristle attached at
{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. But we must not forget the semi-infinite nature
of that bristle, which we find difficult to visually express in this setting.

for
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq 1}
.

3.
Local Abel-Jacobi morphisms

3.1.

In this section, we collect some standard results
about local Abel-Jacobi maps and (simplified)
Contou-Carrère pairings for use in §
5
.
This material is standard and included for the reader’s
convenience.

3.2.

Let
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\in\mathsf{IndSch}
denote the formal disc

\operatorname{Spf}(k[[t]])\coloneqq\operatorname{colim}_{n}\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]]/t^{n})
.

Let
\widehat{\Delta}:\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{D}}
denote the
graph of the canonical map
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.
Clearly this map is a closed embedding (in particular, schematic).

We define:

{\mathcal{O}}(-\widehat{\Delta})\coloneqq\operatorname{Ker}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\to\widehat{\Delta}_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}})\big)\in\mathsf{QCoh}(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{D}}).

One readily checks
that
{\mathcal{O}}(-\widehat{\Delta})
is a line bundle on

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{D}}
: in fact,
a choice of coordinate on the disc
gives a trivialization of it.
Moreover, as:

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\underset{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{D}}}{\times}(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times\mathring{{\mathcal{D}}})=\emptyset

there is a canonical trivialization
of

{\mathcal{O}}(-\widehat{\Delta})|_{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times\mathring{{\mathcal{D}}}}
.

Thus, we obtain a map:

\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}:\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}.

We
define the
local Abel-Jacobi map

\operatorname{AJ}
as
\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}
composed
with the inversion map
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
.

Explicitly, we have:

\begin{gathered}\operatorname{AJ}:\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\\
\tau\mapsto({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(\tau),1)\end{gathered}

where
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(\tau)
is the line bundle on
the disc with sections having at worst simple
poles at
\tau\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}

and the evident trivialization “
1
” on
\mathring{{\mathcal{D}}}
.

3.3.

We now consider a closely related map to the Abel-Jacobi map.

We have an evident evaluation map:

(3.3.1)

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

that is linear in the second coordinate.
Using the non-degeneracy of the residue pairing,
we deduce that there is a unique map:

(3.3.2)

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt.

such that the composition:

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:dlog-aj}\times\operatorname{id}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\xrightarrow{(f,\omega)\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(f\omega)}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

recovers the evaluation map above.

Remark 3.3.0.1
.

Explicitly, (
3.3.2
) is given in coordinates
by:

(\tau\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}})\mapsto\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{i}}{t^{i+1}}dt.

We have the following elementary calculation.

Lemma 3.3.0.2
.

The map (
3.3.2
) coincides with the composition:

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{AJ}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\xrightarrow{-d\log}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt.

Proof.

Choose coordinates as in Remark
3.3.0.1
.
The map
\operatorname{AJ}:\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
lifts to a
map to
{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
via
\tau\mapsto\frac{1}{t-\tau}
.
We then have:

d\log\frac{1}{t-\tau}=\frac{d\frac{1}{t-\tau}}{\frac{1}{t-\tau}}=-\frac{dt}{t-\tau}=-\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\tau^{i}}{t^{i}}dt

Comparing to Remark
3.3.0.1
, we obtain the
claim.

∎

Remark 3.3.0.3
.

Following the lemma, we denote the above map by

-d\log\operatorname{AJ}
, and its additive inverse by
d\log\operatorname{AJ}
.

3.4.
Compatibility with truncations

We use the following observation. We remind that

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}(k[[t]]/t^{n})
.

Clearly
d\log\operatorname{AJ}
maps
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
maps
into
{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
.
Therefore, by definition (see §
2.3.8
),
we deduce that
\operatorname{AJ}
maps
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
into

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
.

3.5.
The positive Grassmannian

Next, we define:

{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\coloneqq{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}{\times}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}.

There is an evident commutative monoid
structure on
{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
and homomorphism

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

We then define:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}\coloneqq{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\operatorname{Ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}).

Explicitly,
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}
parametrizes
the data of
{\mathcal{L}}
a line bundle on the disc and

\sigma\in\Gamma({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{L}})
a (regular) section
such that
\sigma|_{\mathring{{\mathcal{D}}}}
trivializes
{\mathcal{L}}
.

3.6.

For
n\geq 0
, we define:

\operatorname{Sym}^{n}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spf}(k[[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]])^{S_{n}}\in\mathsf{IndSch}.

for
S_{n}
the symmetric group.
We then let:

\operatorname{Sym}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\coloneqq\coprod_{n}\operatorname{Sym}^{n}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\in\mathsf{IndSch}.

3.7.

As for usual smooth curves,
\operatorname{Sym}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}
parametrizes
effective Cartier divisors on
{\mathcal{D}}
supported on
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
Hence, we obtain an isomorphism:

\operatorname{Sym}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}

such that the composition:

\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}=\operatorname{Sym}^{1}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Sym}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\simeq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}\subseteq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

is
\operatorname{AJ}
.

This is an isomorphism of commutative monoids for the
evident product on
\operatorname{Sym}(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}})
.

3.8.
Log Contou-Carrére

3.8.1.

We now observe the following.

There is a unique pairing:

\langle-,-\rangle:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

that is bilinear for multiplicative monoid
structures on both sides and whose restriction
to
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
is
(
3.3.1
).

Explicitly, for
D\in\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}
an effective
Cartier divisor
on
{\mathcal{D}}
supported on
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}
and

f\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, we have:

\langle D,f\rangle=f(D)\coloneqq\operatorname{Nm}_{D}(f).

3.8.2.

There are some variants of the pairing above.

First, note that

\langle-,-\rangle|_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

maps into
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. As
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
is the group completion
of
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}
(in the evident sense), we obtain a
pairing:

\langle-,-\rangle:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

This pairing is compatible with Contou-Carrére’s
pairing
[
CC
]
in the evident sense.

3.8.3.

Next, let
\operatorname{Sym}^{m}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\otimes m,S_{m}})
, which is an
affine scheme. We let
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\coloneqq\coprod_{m}\operatorname{Sym}^{m}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
.

The evident map:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\to\coprod_{m}\operatorname{Sym}^{m}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

maps into
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
. Indeed,
this map is a map of monoids, so the same is
true of its composition with
d\log:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
. As
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
maps into

{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
(cf. §
3.4
),
we obtain the claim by definition of
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
.

For similar reasons, the pairing:

\langle-,-\rangle:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

factors through a pairing:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

that we also denote by
\langle-,-\rangle
.

Remark 3.8.3.1
.

The base-point
0\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}
gives a point of

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}
(of degree 1), hence
a translation map
T:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}
.
There is a natural map:

\operatorname{colim}\mskip 3.0mu(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\xrightarrow{T}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\xrightarrow{T}\ldots)\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}

that we claim is an isomorphism.
Indeed, the assertion is evident at the level of
k
-points (both sides
are
{\mathbb{Z}}
), so it suffices to check the assertion on tangent complexes,
where it is straightforward (both sides naturally identify with

t^{-n+1}k[[t]]/k[[t]]
).

3.8.4.

We will use the following constructions (in a quite simple setting).

Let
V
be a vector space. We consider
V
as a prestack
39
39

39

If
Y
is
coconnective, this prestack is a (DG) indscheme.

with functor of points
\operatorname{Spec}(A)\mapsto\Omega^{\infty}(A\otimes V)\in\mathsf{Gpd}
.

Now suppose
X
is an ind-proper indscheme equipped with a morphism

\varphi:X\to V
. We obtain a canonical “integration” map:

(3.8.1)

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(X,\omega_{X})\to V.

Indeed, our map
\varphi
is (tautologically) equivalent to
a morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to V\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\in\mathsf{QCoh}(X)
. Tensoring with the dualizing
sheaf on
X
, we obtain a morphism
\omega_{X}\to V\otimes\omega_{X}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}(X)
,
or what is the same, a map
p^{!}(k)\to p^{!}(V)
for
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
the projection.
By ind-properness and adjunction,
we obtain a map
p_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}p^{!}(k)\to V
as desired.

Remark 3.8.4.1
.

Using
[
GR2
]
§II.3 Lemma 3.3.7, one finds that this is a bijection;
i.e., specifying a map (
3.8.1
) is equivalent to giving

X\to V
.

We also have the following variant.
Suppose we are also given
Y
a prestack and

\pi:X\times Y\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
a map.

The map
\pi
can be encoded by a morphism
X\to\Gamma(Y,{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
(thinking of the target
as a prestack as above). Therefore,
we obtain a comparison map:

c_{\pi}:\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(X,\omega_{X})\to\Gamma(Y,{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\in\mathsf{Vect}

by the above. (As in Remark
3.8.4.1
,
one can actually recover
\pi
from
c_{\pi}
.)

3.8.5.

We now have the following non-degeneracy assertion for our
pairings.

Proposition 3.8.5.1
.

Each of the morphisms:

\begin{gathered}\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}})\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\\
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}})\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})\\
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\\
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}})\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\end{gathered}

coming from our pairings and §
3.8.4

is an isomorphism.

Moreover, each isomorphism is a map of commutative algebras,
where the left hand sides are commutative algebras using
the commutative monoid structures on the relevant spaces,
while the right hand sides are commutative algebras
as functions on schemes.

Proof.

Each of these maps is a map of commutative algebras
because of the bilinearity of the pairings.

Next, we have:

\begin{gathered}\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})=\underset{m}{\oplus}\mskip 3.0mu\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Sym}^{m}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Sym}^{m}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})=\\
\underset{m}{\oplus}\mskip 3.0mu((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\otimes m,S_{m}})^{\vee}=\operatorname{Sym}((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee})\end{gathered}

where the second equality is by finiteness of

\operatorname{Sym}^{m}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
.

We similarly have

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Sym}((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}))
by
definition. Under these identifications, it suffices to
show that the map:

\operatorname{Sym}((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee})=\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})=\operatorname{Sym}((k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee})

from the proposition is the identity:
it suffices to check this on
the generators of the left hand side (as we have a map
of commutative algebras), and there it follows
by construction of the pairing.

Passing to the colimit as
n\to\infty
, we recover
the first map under consideration.

The second and fourth cases follow from Remark
3.8.3.1
.

∎

Remark 3.8.5.2
.

The assertion
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})
via this
pairing is standard from Contou-Carrère
[
CC
]
.
The other assertions
are apparently less well-known,
40
40

40

For example,
they concern Cartier duality for commutative monoids,
which is less often considered than the group case.

though they easy results.

4.
Coherent sheaves on
{\mathcal{Y}}

4.1.
Overview

In this section, we define
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}
for
{\mathcal{Y}}
and its relatives.
This section follows
[
Ras7
]
§6, though we keep our exposition largely
independent of
loc. cit
.

We strive to keep our exposition as explicit as possible, so assume
whatever simplifying assumptions we like. We refer to
loc. cit
. for
a more thorough development of the subject.

4.2.
Affine case

4.2.1.

Suppose
S
is an eventually coconnective (e.g. classical) affine scheme.

We define the (non-cocomplete, DG) subcategory
\mathsf{Coh}(S)\subseteq\mathsf{QCoh}(S)

to consist of objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{+}
such that for each
n
, the object

\tau^{\geq-n}({\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{\geq-n}
is compact.
In other words,
{\mathcal{F}}
should be eventually coconnective and

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}(S)}({\mathcal{F}},-)
should commute with
filtered colimits in
\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{\geq-n}
for each
n
.
Clearly
\mathsf{Perf}(S)\subseteq\mathsf{Coh}(S)
.

Notation 4.2.1.1
.

For
S=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
, we also sometimes write
\mathsf{Coh}(A)\subseteq A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
for
the subcategory
\mathsf{Coh}(S)\subseteq\mathsf{QCoh}(S)\simeq A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
.

We define
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)
as
\mathsf{Ind}(\mathsf{Coh}(S))
.
Observe that we have a
natural functor
\Psi:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\to\mathsf{QCoh}(S)
.

Define
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{\leq 0}
as the subcategory generated
under colimits by coherent objects that are connective (with respect to the

t
-structure on
\mathsf{QCoh}(S)
). There is a corresponding

t
-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)
with this as its connective subcategory.

Lemma 4.2.1.2

(
[
Ras7
]
Lemma 6.4.1)
.

The above functor
\Psi
is
t
-exact and induces an equivalence

\Psi:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{+}
.

4.2.2.
Pullbacks

Let
f:S\to T
be an eventually coconnective morphism of affine, eventually
coconnective schemes.

4.2.3.
Pushforwards

Now suppose
f:S\to T
is a morphism of affine, eventually coconnective schemes.
In this case, there is a unique continuous DG functor:

f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)

fitting into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(T)}

and such that
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\mathsf{Coh}(S))\subseteq\mathsf{QCoh}(T)^{+}
.
(Indeed, this functor is necessarily the ind-extension of

\mathsf{Coh}(S)\xrightarrow{f_{*}}\mathsf{QCoh}(T)^{+}\simeq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)^{+}\subseteq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)
.

In general, the functor
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
can
41
41

41

Unfortunately,
[
Ras7
]
, which
is currently being revised, erroneously claims otherwise.
be pathological, i.e., it may fail to map
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{+}
into
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)^{+}
.
This issue complicates the theory, compared to the finite type setting.

4.2.4.

Suppose
f:S\to T
as above is eventually coconnective (i.e., of finite

\operatorname{Tor}
-amplitude). In this case,

f^{*}:\mathsf{QCoh}(T)\to\mathsf{QCoh}(S)
maps
\mathsf{Coh}(T)
to
\mathsf{Coh}(S)
. By ind-extension,
we obtain a functor
f^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)
.
It is immediate to see that it is left adjoint to
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
.

Moreover,
f^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is manifestly right
t
-exact. Therefore,

f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}

is
left
t
-exact in this case.

4.2.5.

Suppose
f:S\to T
is proper (e.g., an almost finitely presented
closed embedding).

Then
f_{*}:\mathsf{Coh}(S)\to\mathsf{QCoh}(T)^{+}
maps into
\mathsf{Coh}(T)
. Therefore,
the functor
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
admits a continuous right adjoint,
that we denote
f^{!}
in this case.

4.2.6.
Semi-coherence

We now provide a general hypothesis that ensures that
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is
left
t
-exact, even when
f
is not eventually coconnective.

Definition 4.2.6.1
.

A connective and eventually coconnective
commutative algebra
A
is
semi-coherent

if every
M\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{+}
can be written as a filtered colimit
of objects
M_{i}
such that:

•

M_{i}\in\mathsf{Coh}(A)
for every
i
.

•

There exists an integer
r
independent of
i

such that every
M_{i}
lies in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r}
.

We say
S=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
is
semi-coherent
if
A
is.

Remark 4.2.6.2
.

In the definition, if one restricts to
M\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
,
one finds that such an
r
may be constructed (if it exists)
independent of
M
(by considering direct sums of modules).
It follows that it suffices to check the hypothesis just for
M\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

Example 4.2.6.3
.

Any Noetherian (and eventually coconnective)

A
is obviously semi-coherent. More generally, any
coherent (and eventually coconnective)
A
is semi-coherent.

We now have the following simple observation.

Lemma 4.2.6.4
.

Suppose
S
is a semi-coherent eventually connective affine scheme.
Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}
is a DG category with a
t
-structure
compatible with filtered colimits.
Suppose
F:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a continuous left
t
-exact DG functor
such that
F(\mathsf{Coh}(S))\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}^{+}
.
Then
F(\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{+})\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}^{+}
.

Proof.

Suppose
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{+}
. By assumption and
S
, we can write

{\mathcal{F}}
as a filtered colimit
\operatorname{colim}_{i}{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
with

{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\mathsf{Coh}(S)\cap\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{\geq-r}
for
some
r
; equivalently,
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\mathsf{Coh}(S)\cap\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{\geq-r}
.
Then
F({\mathcal{F}})=\operatorname{colim}_{i}F({\mathcal{F}}_{i})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\geq-r}
by assumption, giving
the claim.

∎

Corollary 4.2.6.5
.

Suppose
f:S\to T
is a morphism of eventually coconnective affine schemes
with
S
semi-coherent. Then
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is
t
-exact.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.2.6.4
,
f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is left
t
-exact.
Therefore, we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(S)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(T)^{+}}

in which the left arrows are
t
-exact equivalences and the
bottom arrow is
t
-exact (by affineness). This gives the claim.

∎

4.2.7.

We have the following important technical result.

Proposition 4.2.7.1
.

The (classical) commutative algebra
A_{n}
defined in §
2.10
is
semi-coherent.

In other words,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
is semi-coherent.

We defer the proof of the proposition to §
4.9
,
at the end of this section. Until that point, we assume it.

4.3.
Coherent sheaves with support

4.3.1.

We now consider the following situation.

Let
Z
be an affine, eventually coconnective scheme.
Let
f:Z\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
be a function. Let
U=\{f\neq 0\}\subseteq Z
,
and let
j:U\to Z
be the embedding.
Let
i:Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z
be a closed subscheme with

Z\setminus Z_{0}=U
.

The following type of result is standard in the finite type setting.

Lemma 4.3.1.1
.

Suppose that:

•

i
is almost finitely presented.

•

The map
i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z_{0})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z)
is
t
-exact.

Then the functor:

(j^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}},i^{!}):\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z)\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(U)\times\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z_{0})

is conservative.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z)
be given. We suppose
{\mathcal{F}}
is a non-zero
object with
j^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}})=0
. Our goal is to construct
an object
{\mathcal{H}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z_{0})
and a non-zero map

i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{H}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
.

Step 1
.

We have:
42
42

42

Indeed,
j_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is automatically left
t
-exact
(hence
t
-exact) because
j
is flat; therefore, this formula follows from
the quasi-coherent setting by ind-extension.

j_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}j^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}})=\operatorname{colim}\big({\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{f}{\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{f}\ldots\big)=0.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathsf{Coh}(Z)
be given with a non-zero map
\alpha:{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
; such

({\mathcal{G}},\alpha)

exists because
{\mathcal{F}}
is assumed non-zero.

By compactness of
{\mathcal{G}}
, there is an integer
n
such that the composition:

{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{f^{n}}{\mathcal{F}}

is null-homotopic. This map coincides with the composition:

{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{f^{n}}{\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}

so this map must be null-homotopic as well.
It follows that
\alpha
factors through a (necessarily non-zero) map:

{\mathcal{G}}/f^{n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Coker}(f^{n}:{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{G}})\to{\mathcal{F}}

for some
n\gg 0
.

We have a standard co/fiber sequence:

{\mathcal{G}}/{f^{n-1}}\xrightarrow{f}{\mathcal{G}}/f^{n}\to{\mathcal{G}}/f.

By descending induction, we see that there must exist
a non-zero map from
{\mathcal{G}}/f\to{\mathcal{F}}
. Replacing
{\mathcal{G}}
with
{\mathcal{G}}/f\in\mathsf{Coh}(Z)
,
we can assume that
f
acts by zero on our original object
{\mathcal{G}}
.

Step 2
.

We now digress to make the following simple commutative algebra
observation.
43
43

43

In our eventual application,
Z_{0}
is the classical
scheme underlying
\{f=0\}
. This step is unnecessary under that
additional hypothesis.

Suppose
Z=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
below.

Let
I\subseteq H^{0}(A)
be the ideal of
elements vanishing on
Z_{0}^{cl}
. Let
(f)\subseteq H^{0}(A)

be the ideal generated by
f
. We claim that there are integers
r,s

such that:

I^{r}\subseteq(f),(f^{s})\subseteq I.

For any
g\in I
, we have
\{g\neq 0\}\subseteq\{f\neq 0\}
by assumption.
Therefore,
f
is invertible in
H^{0}(A)[g^{-1}]
, so
g^{r_{0}}\in(f)
for some

r_{0}
(depending on
g
).
Because
i
is almost finitely presented,
I
is finitely generated,
so we obtain
I^{r}\subseteq(f)
for
r\gg 0
.

The other inclusion (which we do not need) follows similarly:
\{f\neq 0\}
is covered
by
\{g\neq 0\}
for
g\in I
, so
1\in I[f^{-1}]=H^{0}(A)[f^{-1}]
,
so
f^{s}\in I
for some
s
.

We deduce the following: any module:

M\in H^{0}(A/f)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}\subseteq H^{0}(A)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}

admits a finite filtration:

0=I^{r}M\subseteq I^{r-1}M\subseteq\ldots\subseteq IM\subseteq M

with subquotients lying in
H^{0}(A)/I\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}\subseteq A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

Step 3
.

We now return to earlier setting.

Recall that we have
{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathsf{Coh}(Z)
on which
f
acts null-homotopically.
Because
{\mathcal{G}}
is bounded, it has a finite Postnikov filtration as an object
of
\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)

with associated graded terms
H^{i}({\mathcal{G}})[-i]
.
Each
H^{i}({\mathcal{G}})\in\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)^{\heartsuit}
. Because
f
acts by zero on them, they lie
in
\mathsf{QCoh}(\{f=0\})^{\heartsuit}
. By the previous step, each
of these terms is obtained by successively extending
objects in
\mathsf{QCoh}(Z_{0})^{\heartsuit}
.

Now the diagram:

\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}(Z_{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}(Z)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(Z_{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{QCoh}(Z)^{+}}

commutes because we assumed
i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}

t
-exact;
moreover, the vertical arrows are equivalences. Therefore,
{\mathcal{G}}

admits a finite filtration as an object of
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(Z)

with associated graded terms
\mathsf{IndCoh}
-pushed forward from
Z_{0}
.
Therefore, one of these associated graded terms must have a non-zero map
to
{\mathcal{F}}
, giving the claim.

∎

4.3.2.
Application to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}

We now apply the above to construct generators of
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n})
.

Proposition 4.3.2.1
.

For

n>0
, the DG category
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n})
is compactly
generated by the objects

\{\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}})\}_{r\geq 0}
.

For
n=0
, the DG category
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 0})
is compactly
generated by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 0}})
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.

Step 1
.

The
n=0
case is simple:
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 0}
is
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
(with coordinate

a_{0}
).

Step 2
.

We now fix
n>0
, and that the proposition for
n-1
.
We introduce the following notation.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n})
be the subcategory generated
by the objects
\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}}
. We need
to show that
{\mathcal{C}}
is the full
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}
.

Step 3
.

We observe that (
2.8.1
) implies that

\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n-1})\in{\mathcal{C}}
.
More generally, we find

\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n-1})\in{\mathcal{C}}
for
any
r\geq 0
.

Step 4
.

Below, we will apply Lemma
4.3.1.1
with

i:Z_{0}\to Z
corresponding to

\delta_{n}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n-1}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}
. We remark
that
\delta_{n}
is almost finitely presented by
Lemma
2.6.6.2
(
2
)
(cf. the proof of Corollary
2.6.6.3
), and that
\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}

is
t
-exact by Proposition
4.2.7.1
.

In other words, our application of the lemma is justified.

Step 5
.

We now treat the
n=1
case.

Suppose
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1})
lies in the
right orthogonal to
{\mathcal{C}}
.

By Step
3
and the
n=0
case,
\delta_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})=0
, and

\delta_{1}^{!}(\iota^{r})^{!}{\mathcal{F}}=0
more generally. Therefore,
by Lemma
4.3.1.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}
is
*
-extended from:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}\setminus\big(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 0}\cup\ldots\cup\iota^{r}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 0})\big)

for each
r
. It follows that
{\mathcal{F}}
is
*
-extended from:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{1}\coloneqq\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq 1}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}}{\mathcal{U}}_{1}.

But by Lemma
2.9.2.1
(cf. §
2.9.3
),

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{1})\simeq\mathsf{QCoh}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{1})

is generated by its structure sheaf. Since this is the
*
-restriction
of the structure sheaf on
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 1}
, we obtain the claim.

Step 6
.

We now assume
n>1
case.

In this case, we have:

\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\simeq\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}.

(Here we use
\iota
to denote the map for both
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

and
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n-1}
.)

By induction and Step
3
,
{\mathcal{C}}
contains the subcategory
generated under colimits by the essential image of

\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
. Therefore, by Lemma
4.3.1.1
,
any
{\mathcal{F}}
in the right orthogonal to
{\mathcal{C}}
is
*
-extended
from
\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{n}
.

But again,
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{n})\simeq\mathsf{QCoh}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{n})
is generated
by its structure sheaf by Lemma
2.9.1.1
; as

{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, we obtain the claim.

∎

4.4.
Stacky case

4.4.1.

Now suppose
S
is a stack of the form
T/G
for
G
classical affine group scheme
and
T
an eventually coconnective affine scheme.

In this case,
G
acts weakly on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)
, i.e.,
\mathsf{QCoh}(G)

acts canonically on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)
(by flatness of
G
over a point).
We set
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\coloneqq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)^{G,w}
.

There is a unique
t
-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)
such that the
forgetful functor:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(S)\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(T)

is
t
-exact.

4.4.2.

In the above setting, given a map
f:S_{1}\to S_{2}
of stacks of the
above types, we obtain similar functoriality as in the non-stacky case;
we omit the details.

4.4.3.

We now have:

Corollary 4.4.3.1
.

The category
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
is compactly generated
by the objects
\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})(m)
, where
r\geq 0
and

m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

For
G
an affine algebraic group (in particular, of finite type) and

{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
, it is a general fact that
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact
if and only if its image
\operatorname{Oblv}({\mathcal{F}})\in{\mathcal{C}}
is compact.
44
44

44

See

[
Ras7
]
, proof of Lemma 5.20.2.

Moreover, the functor
\operatorname{Oblv}:{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}\to{\mathcal{C}}
always generates
the target under colimits.

Therefore, writing
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
,
the above objects are indeed compact. The generation follows from
Proposition
4.3.2.1
.

∎

4.4.4.

There is a natural functor:

\Psi:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

obtained by passing to weak
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-invariants for
the corresponding functor for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
. This functor
is an equivalence on coconnective (equivalently: eventually coconnective)
subcategories via the corresponding assertion for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
.

We let
\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\subseteq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

denote the subcategory of compact objects. By the above,
the functor
\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

induced by
\Psi
is fully faithful; its essential image is
exactly the subcategory of
eventually coconnective almost compact objects.

4.4.5.

We now observe that the functor:

\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

is
t
-exact.

Indeed, by definition, this assertion reduces to the analogous
statement for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}
, which in turn follows
from Proposition
4.2.7.1
.

The same analysis applies for pushforward functors:

\delta_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}).

4.5.
Ind-algebraic stacks

We define:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})\coloneqq\operatorname{colim}\mskip 3.0mu\big(\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}\ldots\big)\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}.

We let:

i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})

denote the structural functor.

Similarly, we define:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})\coloneqq\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})=\underset{\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}},\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}{\underset{n,m}{\operatorname{colim}}}\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}.

By the above and
[
Ras6
]
Lemma 5.4.3, there is a unique

t
-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})
such that
each structural functor:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})

is
t
-exact. Similarly, there is a unique
t
-structure on

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})
such that each structural functor:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})

is
t
-exact.

Each of these categories is compactly generated as all of our structural
functors preserve compact objects.

4.6.
Grassmannian actions

Suppose
n>0
in what follows.

4.7.

Recall that
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
tautologically acts on
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
.

Observe that
{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
is preserved
under the action of the submonoid

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}\subseteq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
.

Indeed, suppose more generally that
Y
is a prestack
mapping to
{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. Then:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}=\operatorname{Ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})

acts canonically on:

Z\coloneqq Y\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

compatibly with the canonical
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}=\operatorname{Ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-action on:

Y_{\log}\coloneqq Y\underset{{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

Taking
Y={\mathcal{Y}}
, we recover the claim.

4.7.1.

Next, observe that the action of

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\subseteq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}

on
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
preserves
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.

Indeed, this is an immediate consequence of the above.

4.7.2.

By the above, there is a canonical action:

\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})\curvearrowright\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

where the left hand side is given its convolution monoidal
structure.

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{Coh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})\subseteq\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})
,
the corresponding action functor:

\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

clearly preserves
\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
. Therefore, we obtain
a canonical action:

\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})\curvearrowright\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}).

4.7.3.

By Remark
3.8.3.1
, we have:

\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})\underset{\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})}{\otimes}\simeq\big(\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}\ldots\big)\simeq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}).

Thus, we obtain a natural action of
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})

on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})
such that the functor

i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is a morphism of
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})
-module
categories.

4.7.4.

The map
\delta_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1})

is naturally a map of
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n})
-module categories,
as is evident by again considering subcategories of compact objects.

Therefore,
\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})
naturally acts on
\mathsf{IndCoh}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})
,
compatibly with the above actions.

4.8.
Ind-coherent sheaves on
{\mathcal{Y}}

We now define:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\coloneqq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},w}.

By definition, the right hand side is:

\mathsf{Hom}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(\mathsf{Vect},\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})).

We let:

\operatorname{Oblv}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})

denote the (conservative) forgetful functor.
It admits a left adjoint:

\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},w}.

The resulting monad on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})
is easily seen to be

t
-exact.
45
45

45

This reduces to the assertion that for

{\mathcal{F}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n,pos})^{\heartsuit}
, the
action functor
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

is
t
-exact. Filtering
{\mathcal{F}}
, we are reduced to the case that
it is the skyscraper sheaf at a
k
-point. In that case, the
relevant functor is a composition of functors
\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
,
so
t
-exact by Proposition
4.2.7.1
.

Therefore, we obtain:

Proposition 4.8.0.1
.

There is a unique
t
-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
such that the
functor
\operatorname{Oblv}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})
is
t
-exact.
Moreover, the functor
\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},w}
is
t
-exact.

Indeed, this follows from:

Lemma 4.8.0.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}
be equipped with a
t
-structure compatible with
filtered colimits and a right
t
-exact monad
T:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then
T\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}({\mathcal{C}})
admits a unique
t
-structure such that the
forgetful functor
T\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{C}}
is
t
-exact.

4.8.1.

In the truncated setting, we similarly define:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})\coloneqq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}.

We again have adjoint functors:

\operatorname{Oblv},\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}

and a
t
-structure on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})
.

4.8.2.

By functoriality of the constructions, there is a natural functor:

\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}:\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}).

This functor is
t
-exact and preserves compact objects. Moreover, we have:

Lemma 4.8.2.1
.

For
n>0
, the functor
\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
is fully faithful.

Roughly speaking, this is true because the map
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is
formally étale for
n>0
, which follows from the corresponding
fact for
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
(which
follows from Proposition
2.3.4.1
). Unwinding the constructions
to convert this argument into a proof is straightforward.

4.9.
Proof of Proposition
4.2.7.1

We now give the proof of Proposition
4.2.7.1
, which we deferred earlier.

4.9.1.

Our argument is by induction on
n
. We proceed in steps.

4.9.2.
Step 1:
n=0
case

The
n=0
case is trivial, as
A_{0}=k[a_{0}]
is Noetherian.

4.9.3.
Step 2:
n=1
case

The inductive step we give for
n>1
below may be adapted to treat the

n=1
case, but it requires somewhat more work; we indicate how this works
in §
4.9.8
. We prefer
to give a direct argument. Actually, we will show that
A_{1}
is coherent.

Recall the Noetherian subalgebras
A_{1,\leq m}\subseteq A_{1}
from §
2.11
.
It suffices to show that for any finitely presented
M\in A_{1}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
,
there is an integer
m\gg 0
and finitely generated

N\in A_{1,\leq m}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
such that
there is an isomorphism
N\otimes_{A_{1,\leq m}}A_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}M
, where
the tensor product is understood in the derived sense.

Choose some
m_{0}
and
N_{0}\in A_{1,\leq m_{0}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
with an
isomorphism
H^{0}(N_{0}\otimes_{A_{1,\leq m_{0}}}A_{1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}M
; this may be
done by choosing generators and relations for
M
and
m_{0}
such that
the relations all involve linear combinations with coefficients in
A_{1,\leq m_{0}}
.

Choose
m>m_{0}
such that
N_{0}
does not contain any
(b_{-1}-i)
-torsion
for any integer
i\geq m
; this can be done because the finitely generated

A_{1,\leq m_{0}}
-module
N_{0}
only admits finitely many associated primes.
Then it is easy to see that
N\coloneqq H^{0}(A_{1,\leq m}\otimes_{A_{1,\leq m_{0}}}N_{0})
satisfies the hypotheses.

Remark 4.9.3.1
.

We see here that
A_{1}
is actually coherent, not merely semi-coherent.
We do not consider this question for general
n
.

4.9.4.
Step 3: filtered colimits, extensions, and effective bounds

We now make the following observations for dévissage.

For an integer
r
, we say that
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
is
r
-good
if
M
can be
expressed as a filtered colimit of coherent objects concentrated in
degrees
\geq-r
. Clearly any such
M
lies in
A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r}
.

In other words,
M
is
r
-good if it lies in the full subcategory:

\mathsf{Ind}(\mathsf{Coh}(A)\cap A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r})\subseteq A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r}.

Here the natural functor is fully faithful because

\mathsf{Coh}(A)\cap A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r}
consists of objects that are
compact in
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\geq-r}
by assumption.

It follows from the second description that
r
-good objects are closed
under filtered colimits (for fixed
r
). We also observe that
r
-good objects
are closed under extensions.

Finally, we say an object
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
is
good
if it is

r
-good for some
r
. So our task is to show that any
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{+}

is good, or equivalently, any
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

4.9.5.
Step 4: reductions

We now begin our induction. Take
n>1
and assume the result for
n-1
.

Suppose
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{+}
.
It it suffices to show that:

M[b_{-n}^{-1}]\coloneqq\underset{b_{-n}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muM\text{ and }\operatorname{Coker}(M\to M[b_{-n}^{-1}])

are good (where
\operatorname{Coker}
indicates the
homotopy cokernel
,
i.e., the cone).

We check these assertions below. We remark that by Remark
4.2.6.2
,
we are reduced to considering
M\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

4.9.6.
Step 5: generic case

Note that
M[b_{-n}^{-1}]\in A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}]\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}\subseteq A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

As
n>1
, we have an isomorphism:

A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}]=k[b_{-1},\ldots,b_{-n},b_{-n}^{-1}].

by Lemma
2.9.1.1
.

As this algebra is regular Noetherian, it follows that

M[b_{-n}^{-1}]
has bounded
\operatorname{Tor}
-amplitude as an

A_{n}[b_{-n}^{-1}]
-module (simply because it is bounded from below),
so the same is true of
M[b_{-n}^{-1}]
as an
A_{n}
-module.
By standard homological algebra,
M[b_{-n}^{-1}]
can be represented
by a bounded complex of flat (classical)
A_{n}
-modules.
By Lazard, a flat
A_{n}
-module is good, so
M[b_{-n}^{-1}]
is good.

4.9.7.
Step 6: torsion case

We now show that
\widetilde{M}=\operatorname{Coker}(M\to M[b_{-n}^{-1}])
is good.
More generally, we show that any bounded complex

\widetilde{M}\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{+}
such that
b_{-n}
acts locally nilpotently on
its cohomologies is good. As the complex
\widetilde{M}
is assumed bounded,
it suffices to treat its cohomology groups one at a time, so
we can assume (up to shifting) that
\widetilde{M}\in A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
.

By induction, there exists an integer
r
such that any module

N\in A_{n-1}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
is
r
-good as an
A_{n-1}
-module.
Because the projection
A_{n}\xrightarrow{b_{-n}\mapsto 0}A_{n-1}
is
almost finitely presented by Proposition
2.6.6.2
(
2
),
so coherent
A_{n-1}
-complexes restrict to coherent
A_{n}
-complexes,

N
restricts to an
r
-good
A_{n}
-module.

Now let
\widetilde{M}_{i}\subseteq\widetilde{M}
be the
non-derived kernel of the map
b_{-n}^{i}:\widetilde{M}\to\widetilde{M}
.
Clearly
\widetilde{M}_{i}/\widetilde{M}_{i-1}\in A_{n-1}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}\subseteq A_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
, so is
r
-good for the above
r
(which is independent
of everything in sight except
n
). The module

\widetilde{M}_{i}
is then
r
-good, as it is obtained by successively extending

r
-good modules. Finally,
\widetilde{M}=\operatorname{colim}_{i}\widetilde{M}_{i}
is
a filtered colimit of
r
-good modules, so is
r
-good. This concludes the argument.

4.9.8.
Step 7: revisiting the
n=1
case

Finally, we make a remark that the above argument can be adapted to
treat the
n=1
case, if one so desires. As in Lemma
2.9.2.1
,
we should consider the localization of

A_{1}
at
\{b_{-1},b_{-1}-1,b_{-1}-2,\ldots\}

(instead of simply at
b_{-1}
); one again then obtains a Noetherian ring
of finite global dimension. The argument concludes by noting that
any classical
A_{1}/(b_{-1}-i)=k[a_{i}]
-module is
0
-good, so
any module for
A_{1}
that is torsion for the above elements (thus
necessarily the direct sum of its torsion with respect to each) is

0
-good (by an argument as above).

In other words, we need to localize at infinitely many elements before
obtaining a regular ring; the saving
grace is that the goodness for torsion modules at each is bounded independently
of the element.

5.
Spectral realization of Weyl algebras

5.1.
Overview

5.1.1.

Let
i_{n}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
denote the
canonical embedding, as in §
2.7
.

We have
i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\in\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})
,
which lies in
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}
by
Proposition
2.6.5.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}(i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))
, which is a compact object in
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})
.

In this section, we construct an action of
a Weyl algebra in
2n
generators on
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
(considered as an object of

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})
).

5.1.2.

To formulate our construction more canonically, let

W_{n}
denote the algebra of global differential operators
on the scheme
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}\operatorname{Sym}(t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt)
.

Let
W_{n}^{op}
denote
W_{n}
with the reversed multiplication.
We will construct a canonical homomorphism:

(5.1.1)

W_{n}^{op}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\in\mathsf{Alg}=\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Vect}).

Remark 5.1.2.1
.

In Proposition
7.1.1.1
, we will show that this map is actually an isomorphism.

5.2.
Reduction to generators and relations

Note that
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
lies in the heart of the
t
-structure constructed
in Proposition
4.8.0.1
(or rather, its truncated counterpart, as in
§
4.8.1
).

Therefore, the right hand side of (
5.1.1
)
lies in cohomological degrees
\geq 0
.

As the left hand side of (
5.1.1
) is certainly in cohomological
degree
0
, it suffices to construct a homomorphism:

W_{n}^{op}\to\tau^{\leq 0}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})=H^{0}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Vect}^{\heartsuit}).

As both terms are now in degree
0
and the left hand side
has a standard algebra presentation, such a construction
may be given by constructing generators and checking relations.

We need to construct maps:

(5.2.1)

\begin{gathered}t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt=(k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\\
(\omega\in t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt)\mapsto\varphi_{\omega}\end{gathered}

and:

(5.2.2)

\begin{gathered}k[[t]]/t^{n}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\\
(f\in k[[t]]/t^{n})\mapsto\xi_{f}.\end{gathered}

We then need to check that the
\varphi_{\omega}
operators
mutually commute, that the
\xi_{f}
operators mutually commute, and
the identity:
46
46

46

The sign on the right hand side reflects working
with
W_{n}^{op}
, not
W_{n}
.

(5.2.3)

[\xi_{f},\varphi_{\omega}]=-\operatorname{Res}(f\omega)\cdot\operatorname{id}\in H^{0}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}).

We provide these constructions and check these identities in what follows.

5.3.
Action of functions

First, we construct the map
\omega\mapsto\varphi_{\omega}

from (
5.2.1
).

We assume the reader is familiar with the notation
from §
3
below.

5.3.1.

We have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}

where
\alpha=(p_{1},\operatorname{act})
for

\operatorname{act}:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

the action morphism from above.

We have an evident isomorphism:
47
47

47

One can work with

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}
or
\mathsf{QCoh}
for our purposes here.
We chose

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}
for the sake of definiteness.
We also have
written
(-)^{*}
in place of
(-)^{*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}
to keep
the notation simpler. To be clear, this notation refers to
the left adjoint to the
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}
pushforward functor.

\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}=p_{1}^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\simeq\alpha^{*}p_{1}^{*}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

giving a morphism:

\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\alpha_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

Applying
p_{2,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
, we obtain a canonical
morphism:

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to p_{2,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\alpha_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=\operatorname{act}_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

We also have a canonical adjunction morphism

\gamma_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\to\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}
for
\gamma:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
the (ind-closed) embedding.

Pushing forward along
i_{n}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}

and composing, we obtain a canonical morphism:

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\otimes i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\to\operatorname{act}_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}^{\leq n}\boxtimes i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

By Proposition
3.8.5.1
, we have a canonical isomorphism:

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}).

Therefore, by adjunction, the above gives a morphism:
