\begin{gathered}\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}\big(i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}),\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\big)=\\
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}\big(\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\big).\end{gathered}

By
loc. cit
., this is a morphism
of algebras.

For
\omega\in t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt=(k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}
,
there is a corresponding linear function on
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
;
its image under the above map is by definition
\varphi_{\omega}
in
(
5.2.1
). As the above map extended to the symmetric
algebra on this vector space, we have verified that
the operators
\varphi_{\omega}
commute.

Remark 5.3.1.1
.

More evocatively, but a little less rigorously, we have algebra maps:

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}\to{\mathcal{M}}aps_{/{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})

where

and the first map comes by functoriality from the canonical
48
48

48

This

.

map
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to f_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
.

We then obtain an algebra map:

\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})=\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}})\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})

from (

). The detailed construction articulates

5.4.
Action of vector fields

We now define the map
f\mapsto\xi_{f}
from
(
5.2.2
).

5.4.1.

We begin with an elementary observation
involving
C=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x,y]/xy)
.
Let
i:{\mathbb{A}}_{y}^{1}\hookrightarrow C
denote the
embedding of the
y
-axis.

The map:

x\cdot-:{\mathcal{O}}_{C}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}

obviously factors through a map:

i_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{y}^{1}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}

that we also denote
y\cdot-
.

5.4.2.

We now vary to above to incorporate
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-actions.

Consider
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acting on

C
with the first
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
scaling the
x
-axis
and the second
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
scaling the
y
-axis.

The map
y\cdot-
evidently has bidegree

(0,1)
.
Therefore, on the stack
C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, we obtain
the following.

By definition, there are tautological line bundles

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
on
C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

equipped with sections
x\in{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
and

y\in{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
such that

xy=0
as a section of
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
.

From the above, we obtain a canonical map:

(5.4.1)

y\cdot-:i_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{0/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{y}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{2}.

5.4.3.

Before applying the above, we introduce some more
notation.

Let
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg}\subseteq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
denote
the image of
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos}
under the inversion
map
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
.
We use similar notation for
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg,\leq n}
.

Note that
\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}:\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

maps into
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg}
. Similarly,

\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}
maps
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
into

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg,\leq n}
.

We let
\operatorname{act}_{neg}:\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}
denote the action map.
We use evident variants of this as well; crucially,
we also let
\operatorname{act}_{neg}
denote the composition:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}\times i_{n}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{act}_{neg}}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}.

5.4.4.

Now recall from Proposition
2.6.7.2

that we have a canonical map:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

In the notation of §
5.4.2
,
the line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
pulls
back to

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

by construction. The pullback of its section
y

corresponds to the canonical map:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Pol}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt=\Gamma({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})

(with the last term really meaning the
scheme attached to this vector space). We sometimes
denote this section as
\omega^{univ}
in what
follows.

We let

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
denote the fiber
product:

({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\underset{C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}(0/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{y}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}).

(See §
5.4.5
for
an explanation of the notation.)
By Proposition
2.6.7.2
, the
derived fiber product is classical.
49
49

49

This
fact is psychologically convenient, but not literally
necessary in what follows. I.e., a straightforward
restructuring of the discussion that follows could avoid
directly appealing to this fact.
We let

\alpha
denote the embedding of

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
into

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.

Pulling back (
5.4.1
), we obtain
a canonical map:

(5.4.2)

\alpha_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})})\to\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}\simeq\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}.

(As the notation indicates, the

on

is a matter

as opposed to

.)

Remark 5.4.4.1
.

In other words, the section
\omega^{univ}

is scheme-theoretically supported on

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
.

5.4.5.

We now claim that
\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

is the
classical
(not derived!) fiber
product:

(5.4.3)

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})=\Big(({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\Big)^{cl}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}.

Here the morphism

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}

is
\operatorname{act}_{neg}
.

Indeed, we have seen that

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})

is a classical stack, so it suffices to check
the above identity on
A
-points for classical
commutative rings
A
, i.e., we are free to
manipulate our
A
-points in the most naive way.

To calculate
\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
,
we need to recall from
Proposition
2.6.7.2
that
in the notation of §
5.4.2
, the
pullback of the line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
comes from
the evident universal line bundle
on
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}
; i.e.,
its fiber at a point

(\tau,({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}

is
{\mathcal{L}}|_{\tau}
. Its canonical section,
denoted
x
in §
5.4.2
, corresponds
to
s|_{\tau}\in{\mathcal{L}}|_{\tau}
.

Therefore, as a classical prestack,

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

corresponds to those data

(\tau,({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))
with
s\in{\mathcal{L}}(-\tau)
.

Clearly this description exactly matches
(
5.4.3
).

Remark 5.4.5.1
.

The derived version of the
fiber product (
5.4.3
) is easily
seen to differ from

\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
, so our notation
is a bit abusive from our perspective, which
emphasizes derived geometry. (However, we do
not feel so bad about this as we have provided
another derivedly good definition of the space.)

5.4.6.

We now construct a canonical map:

\operatorname{can}:i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes i_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})

as follows.

By the above, we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{act}_{neg}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{n}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}.}

Now applying
i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}

to the evident counit map, we obtain:

\begin{gathered}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\prime,\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{\prime,*,\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\prime,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}=\\
\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\alpha_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}.\end{gathered}

We then applying
\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}

to (
5.4.2
) to obtain a map:

\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\alpha_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})})\to\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

Composing these two maps, we obtain
the desired map
\operatorname{can}
.

5.4.7.

More generally, suppose
f\in k[[t]]/t^{n}
.

There is an associated action map

f\cdot-:\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\to\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n})
.
By functoriality, this gives rise to a morphism:

f\cdot-:\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\to\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

We then define:

\operatorname{can}_{f}:i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes i_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})

as the composition of
\operatorname{can}(=\operatorname{can}_{1})

with
f\cdot-
.

5.4.8.

We now conclude the construction.
Fix
f
as above.

By adjunction,
there is a canonical morphism

\operatorname{AJ}_{*}^{-1,\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\to\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}
.

Therefore,
\operatorname{can}_{f}
gives rise to a morphism:

i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\operatorname{act}_{neg,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes i_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}(i_{n,*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

By adjunction, we obtain the desired
morphism:

\xi_{f}:{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{n}.

5.4.9.

Next, we show that the endomorphisms

\xi_{f}
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
mutually commute.

By construction,
50
50

50

See §
5.5.2
for a much more general
setup.

the assertion immediately reduces to the following one.

Lemma 5.4.9.1
.

The section:

p_{13}^{*}(\omega^{univ})\otimes p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})\in p_{1}^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\otimes p_{2}^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})=\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

is
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariant (for the natural action
permuting the first two factors).

Proof.

There is a canonical morphism:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

given as follows. To a point of the left hand side
defined by

\tau_{1}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
,
\tau_{2}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
,
a line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
and a section

s\in{\mathcal{L}}
, our map assigns the line

{\mathcal{L}}|_{\tau_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}|_{\tau_{2}}
with
its section
s|_{\tau_{1}}\otimes s|_{\tau_{2}}
.

In fact, this map factors through a map:

\operatorname{Sym}^{2}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n})\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

Indeed, for a point
(D,({\mathcal{L}},s))
of the left hand
side, we may regard
D
as a finite subscheme of
{\mathcal{L}}
.
The map then sends the datum to

(\Lambda^{2}(s)\in\Lambda^{2}({\mathcal{L}}|_{D}))\in{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
I.e., this is evident by the usual norm construction.

Unwinding the constructions, this implies the claim.

∎

Remark 5.4.9.2
.

We could have organized the discussion differently.
Instead, we could have generalized our
construction of
\xi_{f}
with
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{neg,\leq n}
replacing

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
via a suitable use of norms (as in the lemma
above). Then we would immediately deduce commutativity of the
operators
\xi_{f}
by the same argument and for the operators

\varphi_{\omega}
.

5.5.
Uncertainty

We now check the remaining Weyl algebra relation
(
5.2.3
).

5.5.1.

First, let us put the above constructions a common framework to allow us to
compute the relevant compositions.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\coloneqq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.
This stack is a groupoid over
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
with its projections

p_{1},p_{2}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
ind-proper.

By adjunction and base-change, morphisms
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
are equivalent to
sections of
p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
. For
51
51

51

For
V\in\mathsf{Vect}
,

v\in V
means
v\in\Omega^{\infty}V
, i.e., we have a point of the
underlying
\infty
-groupoid. If we concretely model
V
as a cochain
complex, we obtain such data from cycles in degree
0
.

\sigma\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n},p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))
,
we let
\psi_{\sigma}:{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
denote the corresponding morphism.

In particular, suppose we are given a prestack
S
with an ind-proper morphism

\eta:S\to{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}
. Given a section of

\eta^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
, we obtain a section of

p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
by functoriality, hence a morphism
as above.

Example 5.5.1.1
.

Let
S={\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
and let

\eta:S\to{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}

be the morphism:

(p_{2},\operatorname{act}\circ(\operatorname{AJ}\times\operatorname{id})):(\tau,({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)\mapsto(({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s),({\mathcal{L}}(\tau),\nabla,s))\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}.

For
\omega\in(k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}\overset{Prop.\ref{p:cc-log}}{=}\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})
, we
obtain the evident section
p_{1}^{*}(\omega)
of:

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}=p_{2}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}=\eta^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

The corresponding map
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is
(
5.2.1
).

Example 5.5.1.2
.

In the notation of §
5.4.4
, let

S=\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.
Let
\eta
be the evident morphism defined using (
5.4.3
).
52
52

52

In the notation of §
5.4.6
, this
morphism is given by
(p_{1}\alpha,\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{\prime})
.

The construction of
\operatorname{can}
(resp.
\operatorname{can}_{f}
) amounted to showing
that the section
\omega^{univ}
(hence
p_{1}^{*}(f)\cdot\omega^{univ}
)
comes from a canonical section of

\eta^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
(i.e., these sections are scheme-theoretically
supported on
\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
). By construction,
the corresponding map
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
is (
5.2.2
).

Remark 5.5.1.3
.

Although

{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}
is not classical, our constructions
here are not sensitive to this. The reason is that
we have a closed embedding
\iota:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

(cf. §
5.5.3
below). As

\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
is ind-finite and

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
is classical,

\Gamma(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))=\Gamma(\iota^{!}(\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))
is a coconnective complex,
and its
H^{0}
is the same for
{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}
and for its
underlying classical stack (both identify with
sections of
\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
scheme-theoretically supported
on
{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n,cl}
).

5.5.2.

We now spell out how to concretely compose morphisms of the above type.

We have three morphisms:

p_{12},p_{23},p_{13}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}.

We remark that
p_{13}
corresponds to the multiplication
on the groupoid.

We begin by claiming that there is a canonical
isomorphism:

(5.5.1)

p_{13}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\simeq p_{12}^{*}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\otimes p_{23}^{*}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

To see this, first observe that

p_{1}p_{13}=p_{1}p_{12}
, so:

p_{13}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=p_{12}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

Next, we have evident isomorphisms:

\begin{gathered}p_{12}^{!}p_{1}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}=p_{12}^{!}(p_{1}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\underset{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}})=p_{12}^{*}p_{1}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\underset{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\leq n}}{\otimes}p_{12}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}}.\end{gathered}

Then the Cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{12}}
\scriptstyle{p_{23}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

yields an isomorphism:

p_{12}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}}=p_{12}^{!}p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}=p_{23}^{*}p_{1}^{!}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}.

Combining the above isomorphims yields (
5.5.1
).

Therefore, given
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
sections of

p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
, we obtain a section:

p_{12}^{*}(\sigma_{1})\otimes p_{23}^{*}(\sigma_{2})

of
p_{13}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})

(using (
5.5.1
)). As
p_{13}
is ind-proper,
we obtain an induced section
\sigma_{1}\star\sigma_{2}

of
p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
.

Then unwinding constructions, we find:

\psi_{\sigma_{2}}\psi_{\sigma_{1}}=\psi_{\sigma_{1}\star\sigma_{2}}.

5.5.3.

To analyze the composition above,
it is convenient to embed our spaces as follows.

First, define a morphism:

\iota:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

as:

{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

where the last arrow is
(p_{2},\operatorname{act})
. Note that
the composition of
\iota
with the projection to
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

is the projection
p_{1}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.

Next, we define a similar morphism:

\widetilde{\iota}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

as:

{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

where this time the last arrow is

(p_{3},\operatorname{act}\circ p_{23},\operatorname{act}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\operatorname{act}))
.

As in Remark
5.5.1.3
, a section of

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n},p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))

is the same as a section of

\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

scheme-theoretically supported on
{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}

(equivalently, on the underlying classical stack).
The same applies for

\widetilde{\iota}
: a section
of
p_{13}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
is the same
as a section of:

\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

scheme-theoretically supported on

{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}
(which is
equivalent to saying at the
classical level).

5.5.4.

Now fix
\omega\in(k[[t]]/t^{n})^{\vee}
and
f\in k[[t]]/t^{n}
.
In effect, §
5.5.2
constructed two sections of

p_{13}^{!}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
, which
induce the compositions

\xi_{f}\varphi_{\omega}
and
\varphi_{\omega}\xi_{f}
(after

).

Let us explicitly describe the resulting sections of

\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
under the
above dictionary.

For
\varphi_{\omega}\xi_{f}
, the corresponding section
is supported on:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\overset{\operatorname{AJ}\times\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}\times\operatorname{id}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}.

The corresponding section of:

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

is
\omega\boxtimes p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot\omega^{univ}
,
with notation as in Examples
5.5.1.1
and
5.5.1.2
.
53
53

53

As
we have shown, this section is scheme-theoretically supported
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\boxtimes\operatorname{act}_{neg}^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
,
hence on
({\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n})^{cl}
.
It is convenient to
rewrite this section as:

p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ}))\in p_{1}^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\otimes p_{23}^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

For
\xi_{f}\varphi_{\omega}
, the corresponding section
is supported on:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}\times\operatorname{AJ}\times\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}.

The corresponding section of:

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

is:
54
54

54

For clarity, we again
highlight that we have shown

({\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n})^{cl}
, as

analysis shows that it is
supported on

(\operatorname{act}_{neg}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times\operatorname{act}))^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})\subseteq({\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\times_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n})^{cl}
.

\begin{gathered}p_{1}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{2}^{*}(\omega)\otimes(p_{1}\times\operatorname{act})^{*}(\omega^{univ}))\in p_{2}^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\otimes(p_{1}\times\operatorname{act})^{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})=\\
\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}.\end{gathered}

Now note that we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}{\times}{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{p_{13}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m\times\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{H}}^{\leq n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}

for

the multiplication on
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
.
As this multiplication is commutative, we see that

\xi_{f}\varphi_{\omega}
could as well have been defined

\begin{gathered}p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes(p_{2},\operatorname{act}\circ p_{13})^{*}(\omega^{univ})).\end{gathered}

This section has the advantage of

.

5.5.5.

We can now conclude the argument. We use the next elementary lemmas.

Lemma 5.5.5.1
.

Let:

\operatorname{cotr}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\otimes\Gamma({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})^{\vee}=\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})

denote the canonical vector.

We then have an equality:

p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})=(p_{2},\operatorname{act}\circ p_{13})^{*}(\omega^{univ}))+p_{12}^{*}(\operatorname{cotr})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}.

Proof.

For convenience, we give the first (resp. second) factor
of our triple product as

{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau_{1}}^{\leq n}
(resp.
{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau_{2}}^{\leq n}
),
where
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
denote the respective coordinates.

For a prestack
S
, note that a section of

\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau_{2}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
is equivalent to
a map
S\to{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
.

For
S={\mathcal{D}}_{\tau_{1}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
,
our sections above correspond to maps:

{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau_{1}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{(\tau_{1},({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\mapsto???}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt

as follows:

\displaystyle p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})

\displaystyle\leftrightsquigarrow(\tau_{1},({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\mapsto\operatorname{Pol}(\nabla)

\displaystyle(p_{2},\operatorname{act}\circ p_{13})^{*}(\omega^{univ}))

\displaystyle\leftrightsquigarrow(\tau_{1},({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\mapsto\operatorname{Pol}(({\mathcal{L}}(\tau_{1}),\nabla,s))=

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\operatorname{Pol}(\nabla)+d\log\operatorname{AJ}(\tau_{1})

\displaystyle p_{12}^{*}(\operatorname{cotr})

\displaystyle\leftrightsquigarrow(\tau_{1},({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\mapsto-d\log\operatorname{AJ}(\tau_{1})

where in the last two lines the map

d\log\operatorname{AJ}
was considered in
§
3.3
-
3.4
; the last line
follows from Lemma
3.3.0.2
.

We clearly obtain the assertion.

∎

We also use the following observations.

Lemma 5.5.5.2
.

The above section
\operatorname{cotr}
of

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}

is scheme-theoretically supported on the diagonally embedded

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}
.

Moreover, for
f\in k[[t]]/t^{n}
and

\omega\in t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt
, the section:

p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes\operatorname{cotr})\in\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}

maps to
\operatorname{Res}(f\omega)\in k
under the
adjunction morphism:

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\to k.

Proof.

Let
A
be a commutative, classical, finite
k
-algebra.

As
\operatorname{id}_{A}:A\to A
is a morphism of
A
-bimodules,
the corresponding element
\operatorname{cotr}\in A^{\vee}\otimes A\in A\otimes A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{\heartsuit}
is scheme-theoretically
supported on the diagonal

\operatorname{Spec}(A)\subseteq\operatorname{Spec}(A)\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
.

Moreover, for
f\in A
and
\omega\in A^{\vee}
, we
have an element
f\otimes\omega\in A\otimes A^{\vee}
.
Tensoring over
A\otimes A
with
A^{\vee}\otimes A
,
we obtain an element:

f\otimes\omega\cdot\operatorname{cotr}\in A^{\vee}\otimes A^{\vee}.

It is easy to see that when we evaluate this
tensor on
1\otimes 1\in A\otimes A
, we obtain

\omega(f)\in k
.

Taking
A=k[[t]]/t^{n}
, we obtain our claim.

∎

By our earlier discussion, the endomorphism

\xi_{f}\varphi_{\omega}-\varphi_{\omega}\xi_{f}
of
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}

corresponds to the section:

p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes(p_{2},\operatorname{act}\circ p_{13})^{*}(\omega^{univ}))-p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ}))

of
\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
.
55
55

55

Of
course we consider

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
mapping to
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

via
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))\mapsto(\operatorname{AJ}(\tau_{1})\cdot\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}(\tau_{2}),({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s))
.

By Lemma
5.5.5.1
, the above section coincides with:

-p_{2}^{*}(f)\cdot p_{12}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}(\omega)\otimes\operatorname{cotr}).

By Lemma
5.5.5.2
, this section is supported
on the diagonally embedded:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\Delta\times\operatorname{id}}{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n},

which evidently
maps to
1\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\subseteq\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

under
p_{13}
. Moreover, Lemma
5.5.5.2
implies that the
pushforward of the above section to
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}=1\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

is:

-\operatorname{Res}(f\omega)\in k\subseteq\Gamma({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\subseteq\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}).

This amounts to (
5.2.3
), concluding
the argument.

6.
Compatibility with class field theory

In this section, we show that the construction of §
5
is compatible
with geometric class field theory in a suitable sense.

6.1.
Conventions regarding
D
-modules

6.1.1.

Before proceeding, we take a moment to establish some
notational conventions regarding
D
-modules.
We refer to
[
GR1
]
for details.

6.1.2.

Let
X
be a smooth variety. As in
loc. cit
.,
we have the so-called
left
forgetful functor

\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}:D(X)\to\mathsf{QCoh}(X)
. It is normalized
so that the functor
\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}[\dim X]

is
t
-exact; for example, this functor sends the dualizing

\omega_{X}
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
(with its standard left
D
-module structure).

6.1.3.

The functor
\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}
admits the left adjoint

\operatorname{ind}^{\ell}
. For us, we take as a definition that the sheaf of
differential operators on
X
is:

D_{X}\coloneqq\operatorname{ind}^{\ell}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}).

We remark that this object is concentrated in degree
-\dim X
.
(On general grounds, it coincides with
\operatorname{ind}^{r}(\omega_{X})
, where

\operatorname{ind}^{r}
is the right
D
-module induction functor and

\omega_{X}=\Omega_{X}^{\dim X}[\dim X]\in\mathsf{QCoh}(X)
is the
dualizing sheaf.)

6.1.4.

Now suppose
X
is smooth and affine. Let
\operatorname{Diff}(X)
be the
algebra of global differential operators on
X
.

As in
[
GR1
]
,
\operatorname{End}_{D(X)}(D_{X})
canonically coincides with

\operatorname{Diff}(X)
, but with reversed multiplication. It follows that there
is a canonical equivalence:

(6.1.1)

\operatorname{Diff}(X)\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\simeq D(X)

between left modules for
\operatorname{Diff}(X)
and the category
D(X)
,
sending
\operatorname{Diff}(X)
to
D_{X}
. We remark that this functor is only

t
-exact up to shift.

6.2.
Construction of functors

6.2.1.

In §
5
, we constructed compact objects

{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})^{c}
and morphisms:

W_{n}^{op}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}).

We obtain a corresponding functor:

\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}:W_{n}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\overset{\eqref{eq:diff-dmod}}{\simeq}D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}

uniquely characterized by sending
D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
to

{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
compatibly with the action of
W_{n}^{op}=\mathsf{End}_{D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})
.

Remark 6.2.1.1
.

Above, we carefully normalized
D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
to lie in cohomological
degree
-n
. This is not especially relevant in our analysis
until §
8
.

6.2.2.

For later use, we also use the notation

\Delta_{n}
to denote the further composition:

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\xrightarrow{\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})\xrightarrow{\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}).

6.3.
Cartier duality

We now review some constructions from geometric class field theory.

6.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
be the
n
th congruence subgroup,
i.e.,
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Ker}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
.

Recall that there is a canonical bimultiplicative pairing:

(6.3.1)

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n})_{dR}\to{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

One basic property is that its restriction along the map:

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n})_{dR}\to{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

factors through the Contou-Carrère duality pairing:

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\to{\mathbb{B}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{\leq n}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\xrightarrow{CC}{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

Here we normalize the sign in the Contou-Carrère pairing
so that we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{CC}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

where the bottom arrow is induced by the pairing of
Proposition
3.8.5.1
.

From (
6.3.1
) and functoriality, we obtain a symmetric
monoidal Fourier-Mukai functor:

(6.3.2)

D({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/{\mathcal{K}}_{n})\to\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}).

Theorem 6.3.1.1
.

The functor (
6.3.2
) is an equivalence.
In particular, there is a canonical fully faithful symmetric monoidal functor

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\hookrightarrow\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})
.

Passing to the limit over
n
, we also obtain the following theorem
of Beilinson-Drinfeld.

Theorem 6.3.1.2
.

There is a canonical symmetric monoidal equivalence:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

6.4.
Formulation of the equivariance property

Now observe that
\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}
maps a category acted on by

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
to one acted on by

\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})
(via the structure map

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
).

Using Theorem
6.3.1.1
, we can regard both sides as
acted on by
D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
.

In the remainder of this section, we show that

\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}
is canonically a morphism of

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories.

6.5.
Digression on Harish-Chandra data

We will construct the equivariance structure using the theory of
Harish-Chandra
data
. We review this theory below.

In what follows,
G
is an affine algebraic group and
A\in\mathsf{Alg}
is a DG algebra.
In our applications,
A
will be classical, so at some points in the discussion we
will assume that.

6.5.1.

First, suppose that
G
acts on
A
as an associative algebra. In other words,
we assume we are given a lift of
A
along the forgetful functor

\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))\to\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Vect})=\mathsf{Alg}
.

This defines a canonical weak action of
G
on
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
. Its basic property is that
the forgetful functor
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to\mathsf{Vect}
is weakly
G
-equivariant.

6.5.2.

Now suppose that
{\mathcal{C}}
is a DG category with a weak
G
-action.

Recall that
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
naturally acts on
{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
.
Therefore, we can consider
{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
as enriched over
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
For
{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
, we let:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathsf{Rep}(G)

denote the corresponding mapping complex.

Remark 6.5.2.1
.

There are natural maps:

\operatorname{Oblv}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\operatorname{Oblv}({\mathcal{F}}),\operatorname{Oblv}({\mathcal{G}}))

that are easily seen to be isomorphisms for
{\mathcal{F}}
compact.

Similarly, for

{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
, we let:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))

denote the inner endomorphism algebra.

6.5.3.

In our earlier setting, suppose we are given a weakly
G
-equivariant
functor:

F:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to{\mathcal{C}}.

Note that
A\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{G,w}
, so we obtain a natural object

{\mathcal{F}}\coloneqq F^{G,w}(A)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
. Moreover, there is a canonical map:

\varphi:A\simeq\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}^{enh}(A)\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G)).

(Here the superscript
op
indicates the reversed multiplication.)

Conversely, given
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
and
\varphi
as above, we obtain a functor

F
as above. Indeed, as is standard, a datum
\varphi
is the
same as specifying a morphism

A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{G,w}=A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))\to{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}

of
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
-module categories; by de-equivariantization
(i.e., tensoring over
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
with
\mathsf{Vect}
), we obtain the
desired functor
F:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

6.5.4.

Next, recall from
[
Ras7
]
§10
that
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))
carries a canonical monad
B\mapsto U({\mathfrak{g}})\#B
.

Here
U({\mathfrak{g}})\#B
is the usual smash (or crossed) product construction.
Non-derivedly, modules for
U({\mathfrak{g}})\#B
are vector spaces equipped
with an action of
B
and an action of
{\mathfrak{g}}
that are compatible under the
action of
{\mathfrak{g}}
on
B
by derivations. For a proper derived construction
(in the more complicated setting of topological algebras), we refer to

[
Ras7
]
.

By definition, making
A\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))
into a module over this monad
is a
Harish-Chandra
datum for
A
. When
A
is classical, this
amounts to the data of a morphism:

i:{\mathfrak{g}}\to A

such that
i
is a
G
-equivariant morphism of Lie algebras such that

[i(\xi),-]:A\to A

coincides with the derivation defined by
\xi\in{\mathfrak{g}}
and the
G
-action on
A
.

From
loc. cit
., it follows that specifying a Harish-Chandra datum for

A
is the same as extending the weak
G
-action on
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
to a strong
G
-action.

6.5.5.

Now suppose that
G
acts strongly on
{\mathcal{C}}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}

be a fixed object. We claim that
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))

carries a canonical Harish-Chandra datum.

The construction is formal. Let
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
be the
(non-cocomplete) DG category generated by
{\mathcal{F}}
under finite colimits and
direct summands. As in
[
Ber
]
, the monoidal category:

{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{C}}_{G}\coloneqq\mathsf{End}_{G\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(D(G)^{G,w})^{op}={\mathfrak{g}}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{G,w}

of Harish-Chandra bimodules acts canonically on
{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
.
Moreover,
{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
is
rigid
monoidal, so its (non-cocomplete)
monoidal subcategory

{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{C}}_{G}^{c}
of compact objects preserves
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
. Therefore,

{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
acts on
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\coloneqq\mathsf{Ind}({\mathcal{D}}_{0})
. By de-equivariantization,

{\mathcal{D}}_{1}={\mathcal{D}}^{G,w}
for a canonical strong
G
-category
{\mathcal{D}}
.
But clearly
{\mathcal{D}}=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
by construction,
so we obtain the Harish-Chandra datum as desired (from §
6.5.4
).

Remark 6.5.5.1
.

In particular, there is a canonical map

{\mathfrak{g}}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})
of Lie algebras in
\mathsf{Rep}(G)
.
This map is the standard
obstruction to strong equivariance

on
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Moreover, we see that if
A\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))
is equipped with a Harish-Chandra
datum, giving a strongly
G
-equivariant functor:

F:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to{\mathcal{C}}

is equivalent to giving
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
and
a map
\varphi:A\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}
that is

compatible with the Harish-Chandra data
.

In the case that
A
is classical and
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}

is coconnective, this simply amounts to saying that composition:

{\mathfrak{g}}\xrightarrow{i}A\xrightarrow{\varphi}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}

coincides with the obstruction to strong equivariance on the object
{\mathcal{F}}
.

6.5.6.

We now add one more mild observation before concluding our discussion. The
reader may safely skip this material and return back to it as necessary.

Suppose
G
acts weakly on
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
.
There is a canonical map:

\operatorname{Oblv}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(\operatorname{Oblv}({\mathcal{F}}),\operatorname{Oblv}({\mathcal{G}}))\in\mathsf{Vect}

that is an isomorphism whenever
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact. (This is the
reason we use the subscript
{\mathcal{C}}
in
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}
and
not
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}}^{enh}
: the underlying vector space
of the representation
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
is
comparable to, and often isomorphic to, the complex of maps
from
{\mathcal{F}}
to
{\mathcal{G}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
itself, not in
{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
.)

Now suppose that we are in the setting of §
6.5.5
.
Suppose
{\mathcal{F}}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
is compact,
A
is classical, and

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})
is coconnective. Then the data
of a map:

A\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}

compatible with the Harish-Chandra data is equivalent to
a map:

\varphi:A\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}\in\mathsf{Alg}=\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Vect})

such that the composition:

{\mathfrak{g}}\xrightarrow{i}A\xrightarrow{\varphi}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}

is the obstruction to strong equivariance. Indeed, this follows
from the assumption that
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact, so
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}
, and the connectedness of
G
, so that
the map
\varphi
will automatically be a morphism of
G
-representations.
(We remark that the co/connectivity assumptions allow us to replace

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}
with the classical algebra

H^{0}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})^{op}
.)

6.6.
Reduction

We now return to our particular setting, and apply the above material to
reduce our construction to a calculation.

6.6.1.

Let
G={\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
act strongly on
{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})

via geometric class field theory. By construction, we have:

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})^{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}\simeq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}).

Under this dictionary, the forgetful functor

{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}\to{\mathcal{C}}
corresponds to the
\mathsf{IndCoh}
-pushforward

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n}
(i.e.,
!
-averaging
for
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
).

6.6.2.

We have the object
i_{n,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})

that maps to
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})
under this forgetful functor.
In §
5
, we constructed the map:

\varphi:A\coloneqq W_{n}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})^{op}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})^{op}.

(We remind that the displayed isomorphism is a formal consequence
of compactness of
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
.)

Now on the one hand, we have a morphism:

\operatorname{Lie}({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},T_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\to W_{n}

encoding the infinitesimal action of
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. We denote this morphism by
\alpha
.

On the other hand, we have the composition:

\operatorname{Lie}({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\simeq(t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt)^{\vee}\subseteq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt})\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}^{enh}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})^{op}

where the last morphism here uses the map:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Pol}}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt.

We denote this morphism by
\beta
.

In §
6.7
, we will prove:

Lemma 6.6.2.1
.

The diagram:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Lie}({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{W_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})}

commutes.

Observe that under Theorem
6.3.1.1
,
\beta
is interpreted
as the obstruction to strong equivariance. Therefore,
from §
6.5.6
, we deduce:

Proposition 6.6.2.2
.

The functor
\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}
is canonically a morphism of

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories.

6.7.
Proof of Lemma
6.6.2.1

6.7.1.

Using the additive structure on
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
,
we compute its global vector fields as:

\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},T_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt).

A straightforward calculation shows that the infinitesimal action
map:

\operatorname{Lie}({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1},T_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt)

which was used in the definition of
\alpha
above,
is the map:

\begin{gathered}(f\in k[[t]]/t^{n})\mapsto(1\otimes f)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}=(f\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}\in k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\\
\subseteq k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt).\end{gathered}

Here we recall the canonical vector:

\begin{gathered}\operatorname{cotr}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\otimes\Gamma({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})^{\vee}=\\
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})=k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\end{gathered}

from Lemma
5.5.5.1
.

Therefore, we have:

\alpha(f)=(1\otimes f)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}\in k[[t]]/t^{n}\otimes t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\subseteq W_{n}.

6.7.2.

To simplify the discussion, we take
f=1
for the time being.
We wish to show that
\varphi\alpha(1)=\beta(1)
, i.e., we wish
to show that
\varphi(\operatorname{cotr})=\beta(1)
.
We will adapt the calculation to discuss the
case of general
f
in §
6.7.7
.

6.7.3.

Let us unwind the construction of
\varphi(\operatorname{cotr})
in this case.

First, we form:

\operatorname{cotr}\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}).

Second, we form:
56
56

56

Here we abuse notation: in §
5.4.4
, we used the
notation
\omega^{univ}
for a similar construction with
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
in place of

{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
; that setting is pulled back from the present
one.

\omega^{univ}\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt,\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt}).

We then form:

p_{12}^{*}(\operatorname{cotr})\cdot p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt,\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt}).

According to §
5.5.4
,
\varphi(\operatorname{cotr})
is calculated as follows.
We pull back the above section to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
, pushforward along:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}\times\operatorname{AJ}\times\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{mult}\times\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

and apply the construction of §
5.5.1
.

Of course, the pullback and the pushforward commute here.
Therefore, in what follows we calculate the pushforward with
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

replaced by
{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
.

In what follows, let:

\sigma\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt,\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt})

denote the resulting section. So our objective, in effect, is to calculate

\sigma
.

6.7.4.

Recall from Proposition
3.8.5.1
that:

(6.7.1)

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

Moreover, this is an isomorphism of commutative algebras. It is
convenient to reinterpret the above construction using this isomorphism.

By construction, note that the composition:

t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\simeq\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n},\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}})\to\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},\omega_{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

using pushforward along
\operatorname{AJ}
sends:

\eta\in t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt

to the function:

(g\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(g\eta).

Similarly, if we use
\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}
instead, we obtain the
function:

(g\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(g^{-1}\eta).

6.7.5.

Our original section:

p_{12}^{*}(\operatorname{cotr})\cdot p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})\in\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt,\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes\omega_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt})

can be interpreted as a map:

\begin{gathered}{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt=\underline{t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt}\to\underline{t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\otimes t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt}\end{gathered}

where for a finite-dimensional vector
space
V
, we let
\underline{V}
denote the scheme
\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Sym}(V^{\vee}))
.
It is immediate to see that the above map is given
by the formula:

\eta\mapsto(\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}.

Pushing
p_{12}^{*}(\operatorname{cotr})\cdot p_{23}^{*}(\omega^{univ})
forward along:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{AJ}^{-1}\times\operatorname{AJ}\times\operatorname{id}}\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt

and applying (
6.7.1
), we obtain a function on

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt.

We deduce that it is given by the formula:

(6.7.2)

(g_{1},g_{2},\eta)\mapsto(\operatorname{Res}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((g_{1}^{-1}\otimes g_{2})\cdot(\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}).

6.7.6.

We now wish to calculate (
6.7.2
) more explicitly.

First, we note the following, which is an immediate calculation.

Lemma 6.7.6.1
.

For
\eta\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
, we have:

(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr})=\eta.

We then deduce:

Lemma 6.7.6.2
.

For
(g,\eta)\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt
, we have:

(\operatorname{Res}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((\eta\otimes g)\cdot\operatorname{cotr})=\operatorname{Res}(g\eta).

Proof.

We have:

(1\otimes g)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}=(g\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}.

Indeed, this encodes the fact that the residue pairing is

k[[t]]
-equivariant.

Therefore, we have:

(\eta\otimes g)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}=(g\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}

which gives:

(\operatorname{Res}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((\eta\otimes g)\cdot\operatorname{cotr})=\operatorname{Res}(\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((g\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}).

Now the result follows from Lemma
6.7.6.1
.

∎

In the setting of (
6.7.2
), we deduce:

(6.7.3)

(\operatorname{Res}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((g_{1}^{-1}\otimes g_{2})\cdot(\eta\otimes 1)\cdot\operatorname{cotr})=\operatorname{Res}(\frac{g_{2}}{g_{1}}\eta).

Now, our section
\sigma
is obtained by using a pushforward
along
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
.
On the dual side, this means we restrict our function along:

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\xrightarrow{\Delta\times\operatorname{id}}{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{pol,\leq n}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}dt.

By (
6.7.3
), we see the resulting function is:

(g,\eta)\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(\eta).

This clearly coincides with the endomorphism defined by
\beta(1)
, giving the
claim (in the
f=1
case).

6.7.7.

As promised, we now treat the case of general
f
.

Briefly, one replaces (
6.7.2
) with:

(g_{1},g_{2},\eta)\mapsto(\operatorname{Res}\otimes\operatorname{Res})((g_{1}^{-1}\otimes g_{2})\cdot(\eta\otimes 1)\cdot(1\otimes f)\cdot\operatorname{cotr}),

which Lemma
6.7.6.2
implies is:

(g_{1},g_{2},\eta)\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(\frac{fg_{2}}{g_{1}}\eta),

which for
g_{1}=g_{2}=g
is:

(g,\eta)\mapsto\operatorname{Res}(f\eta).

Again, the induced endomorphism clearly coincides with that
defined by
\beta(f)
, concluding the argument.

7.
Fully faithfulness

7.1.
Overview

7.1.1.

The main result of this section is the following.

Proposition 7.1.1.1
.

For every
n\geq 0
, the functor
\Delta_{n}
is fully faithful.

Remark 7.1.1.2
.

By Lemma
4.8.2.1
, the pushforward
functors
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{n})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
are fully faithful
for
n>0
. Therefore, for
n\neq 0
, the above result is equivalent
to fully faithfulness of
\overline{\Delta}{}_{n}
.

Remark 7.1.1.3
.

As
\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})
is compact in

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
, Proposition
7.1.1.1
is equivalent to showing
that the map:

W_{n}^{op}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{F}}_{n})

is an isomorphism.

Our proof is essentially by induction. We settle the
n=0
case
by explicit analysis. We then use local class field theory
(or the Contou-Carrère pairing) to perform the inductive step.

7.2.
Proof for
n=0

We begin with the base case of our induction.

7.2.1.
A preliminary calculation

We begin with the following explicit calculation.

Recall the scheme
C=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x,y]/xy)
from §
2.6.3
.
We equip it with the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action of §
2.6.6
,
so
\deg(x)=1
and
\deg(y)=0
for the corresponding grading.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}(C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\heartsuit}
denote the structure
sheaf of the
x
-axis, i.e., the object corresponding to the graded

k[x,y]/xy
-module
k[x]=k[x,y]/y
(where the generator has degree
0
).

Lemma 7.2.1.1
.

The unit map:

k\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}(C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows by a standard
\operatorname{Ext}
-calculation that we give here.

Let
A=k[x,y]/xy
. We let
A(n)
denote
A
as a graded
A
-module
where the generator is given degree
n
.
57
57

57

We remark that the
sign conventions here are the same as in §
2.7.2
.

The complex:

\ldots\xrightarrow{y\cdot-}A(2)\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}A(1)\xrightarrow{y\cdot-}A(1)\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}A\xrightarrow{y\cdot-}\underset{\text{coh. deg. }0}{A}\to 0\to\ldots

gives a graded free resolution of
k[x]=A/y
.

Therefore, we may calculate
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(k[x])
as a
graded vector space via the complex:

\ldots\to 0\to\underset{\text{coh. deg. }0}{k[x]}\xrightarrow{0}k[x]\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}k[x](-1)\xrightarrow{0}k[x](-1)\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}k[x](-2)\xrightarrow{0}\mathinner{\ldotp\ldotp\ldotp\ldotp}

The (graded) degree
0
component of this complex,
which computes:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(k[x])=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}(C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}),

is:

\ldots\to 0\to\underset{\text{coh. deg. }0}{k}\xrightarrow{0}k\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}k\cdot x\xrightarrow{0}k\cdot x\xrightarrow{x\cdot-}k\cdot x^{2}\xrightarrow{0}\mathinner{\ldotp\ldotp\ldotp\ldotp}

Clearly this complex is acyclic outside of degree cohomological
0
,
and its
H^{0}
is 1-dimensional and generated by the unit.

∎

7.2.2.

We have the following result, explicitly describing the geometry of our situation.

Lemma 7.2.2.1
.

(1)

There is a canonical isomorphism
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
Explicitly, given
({\mathcal{L}},\nabla,s)\in{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}
, we
take the line
{\mathcal{L}}|_{0}
with its section
s|_{0}
(for
0\in{\mathcal{D}}
the base-point).

(2)

The canonical map
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is an ind-closed embedding.
Its formal completion
{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}^{\wedge}

is isomorphic to
(C/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\wedge}
compatibly with
the above isomorphism
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}={\mathbb{A}}_{x}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Proof.

We fix a coordinate
t
and then consider
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

as mapping to
{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
via the isomorphism
Proposition
2.3.5.1
.
For a prestack
S
over
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
, let
\widetilde{S}
denote
the base-change
S\times_{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\operatorname{Spec}(k)
. (We remark that this
notation is compatible with that of §
2.7.2
.)

By Proposition
2.6.5.1
and Theorem
2.4.3.1
,

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
are classical. Moreover, the formal completion

{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}^{\wedge}
is classical: this follows immediately
from Proposition
2.6.6.2
(
1
).

We can explicitly calculate
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}^{\wedge}}

by classicalness and Proposition
2.3.5.1
: it parametrizes

y\in{\mathbb{A}}_{0}^{1,\wedge}
(defining the local system
({\mathcal{O}},d-y\frac{dt}{t})
)
and an element
f\in({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}^{\wedge}

such that
df=yf\frac{dt}{t}
.
If we expand
f=\sum a_{i}t^{i}
, we find
ia_{i}=ya_{i}
. As
y
is
nilpotent, this implies
a_{i}=0
for all
i\neq 0
and
ya_{0}=0
.
Clearly
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}
corresponds to the locus
y=0
.
Therefore, writing
x
for
a_{0}
and noting that the relevant
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action
scales
x
, we obtain the claims.

∎

7.2.3.

Now the
n=0
case of Proposition
7.1.1.1
asserts that the unit map:

k\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}({\mathcal{F}}_{0})

is an isomorphism. Note that
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
is just the pushforward
of the structure sheaf of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
. The calculation of
the above depends only on the formal completion, so we
obtain the result from Lemmas
7.2.1.1
and
7.2.2.1
.

7.3.
A fully faithfulness result

Before proceeding to our induction, we will need the following observations.

7.3.1.

Recall the maps
\zeta
and
\widetilde{\zeta}
from §
2.7
.

Proposition 7.3.1.1
.

For any
n>0
, the map:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}})\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(\zeta_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\pi}_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})

is an isomorphism.

We prove this result in §
7.3.6
, after some
preliminary remarks.

7.3.2.

Let
A=\oplus_{i\geq 0}A_{i}
be a

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
-graded (classical, say) algebra.

We let
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}
denote the
DG category of graded
A
-modules. For
M\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
,
we write
M=\oplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}}M_{i}
for its
decomposition into weight spaces.

Lemma 7.3.2.1
.

Suppose
M,N\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
. Suppose that

M
is concentrated in positive (graded) degrees,
i.e.,
M=\oplus_{i>0}M_{i}
. Suppose
N
is concentrated
in degree
0
, i.e.,
N=N_{0}
.

Then:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(M,N)=0.

Proof.

Let
A(i)\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
be as in the proof of Lemma
7.2.1.1
, i.e.,

A
graded with generator in degree
i
. The modules
A(i)
for
i>0

generate the subcategory of
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
consisting of modules concentrated
in positive degrees. So we reduce to the case
M=A(i)
for
i>0
, for which the claim is obvious.

∎

7.3.3.

Suppose now that we are given a
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
-graded
algebra
B=\oplus_{i\geq 0}B_{i}

and a graded map
\iota^{*}:A\to B
inducing
an isomorphism
A_{0}\xrightarrow{\simeq}B_{0}
. We let
\iota_{*}:B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}\to A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}

and
\iota^{*}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}\to B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
denote the induced
adjoint functors.

Proposition 7.3.3.1
.

In the above setting, suppose
M,N\in B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
with

M
concentrated in non-negative graded
58
58

58

As opposed to cohomological.

degrees and
N
concentrated in degree
0
.

Then the natural map:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(M,N)\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(\iota_{*}M,\iota_{*}N)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Step 1
.

Let
\widetilde{M}\in A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}
be concentrated in non-negative graded degrees.
We claim that
\operatorname{Ker}(\widetilde{M}\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\widetilde{M})
is concentrated
in (strictly) positive graded degrees.

First, in the case
\widetilde{M}=A
, this map is
\operatorname{Ker}(A\to\iota_{*}(B)
, in which case
the claim follows as we assumed
A\to B
an isomorphism in graded degree
0
.

In general, we have:

\operatorname{Ker}(\widetilde{M}\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\widetilde{M})=\operatorname{Ker}(A\to\iota_{*}(B))\underset{A}{\otimes}\widetilde{M}

which is the tensor product of a module in graded degrees
\geq 0
and one in graded degrees
>0
, so
is in graded degrees
>0
(as
A
is non-negatively graded), as claimed.

Step 2
.

Next, we claim that
\operatorname{Ker}(\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M\to M)
is concentrated in strictly positive graded degrees.

Clearly
\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M=B\otimes_{A}\iota_{*}M
is in degrees
\geq 0
, so it suffices to see that

\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M\to M
is an isomorphism in degree
0
. We can check this after applying
\iota_{*}
.
Then we have a retraction:

\iota_{*}M\to\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M\to\iota_{*}M,

so it suffices to see that the first map is an isomorphism in degree
0
.
Taking
\widetilde{M}=\iota_{*}M
, this follows from Step
1
.

Step 3
.

Applying Lemma
7.3.2.1
to
\operatorname{Ker}(\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M\to M)
and
N
, we see that the map:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(M,N)\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{B\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(\iota^{*}\iota_{*}M,N)

is an isomorphism. The right hand side identifies with
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}}(\iota_{*}M,\iota_{*}N)

by adjunction, and the induced map is given by
\iota_{*}
functoriality, so we obtain the claim.

∎

7.3.4.

We will apply the above in the following setting.

Let
A_{n}
be the graded ring of §
2.10
.
We write
A_{n}=\oplus_{i\geq 0}A_{n,i}
for
its decomposition into graded pieces. We remind
from
loc. cit
. that
\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
.

By construction, we have:

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\underset{{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}\operatorname{Spec}(k)=\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n,0})

such that the graded algebra maps
A_{n,0}\to A_{n}\to A_{n,0}

correspond to the maps

(7.3.1)

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\zeta}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}

(the latter map being the projection).

7.3.5.

Recall the maps
\iota
,
\iota^{r}
from §
2.7
.

The map
\iota
arises
59
59

59

Non-canonically: i.e., we need a choice
of coordinate on the disc to turn
\iota
into a map of
stacks over
{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
from a map
\iota^{*}:A_{n}\to A_{n}
of graded rings.
This map is an isomorphism
on
0
th graded components, as is evident from
(
7.3.1
).

Corollary 7.3.5.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n})
. Then the morphism:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}{\mathcal{G}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\iota_{*}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}{\mathcal{G}})

is an isomorphism. More generally,
for any
r,s\geq 0
, the morphism:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}(\iota_{*}^{r}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}),\iota_{*}^{s}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}{\mathcal{G}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}(\iota_{*}^{r+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}),\iota_{*}^{s+1}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}{\mathcal{G}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Translating to graded modules and using (
7.3.1
),
the hypotheses of Proposition
7.3.3.1
are clearly satisfied, giving the result.

∎

7.3.6.
Conclusion

We now prove Proposition
7.3.1.1
.

Observe that
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}=\operatorname{lim}_{n}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
, where the limit
is formed under the (affine) morphism
\iota
. Therefore, we have:

\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}=\underset{r}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\iota_{*}^{r}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}).

As all of these terms are in the heart of the
t
-structure,
we obtain a similar identity in
\mathsf{IndCoh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
, using
\mathsf{IndCoh}
-pushforwards
in the notation instead.

Define:

{\mathcal{G}}\coloneqq\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n})^{\heartsuit}.

We let
i:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}=\operatorname{colim}_{n,\iota}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}

denote the structural map. Using adjunction, we rewrite:

(7.3.2)

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(\zeta_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\pi}_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}),

which is the right hand side of Proposition
7.3.1.1
, as:

\begin{gathered}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}(i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}},i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})=\\
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}(i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}},i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}}).\end{gathered}

By the above, we can further express this term as:

\begin{gathered}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}(i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\underset{r}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}})=\\
\underset{r}{\operatorname{lim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}(i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},i_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}}).\end{gathered}

As
\iota_{*}^{r}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\in\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})
for all
r
,
by
[
Ras5
]
Corollary 6.5.3, we may calculate the above as:

\underset{r}{\operatorname{lim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underset{s}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}(\iota_{*}^{r+s,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\iota_{*}^{s}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}}).

Now for fixed
r
, Corollary
7.3.5.1
implies that all of the
maps in the above colimit are isomorphisms. Therefore, the above
may be calculated as:

\underset{r}{\operatorname{lim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}(\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}}).

Moreover, the analysis from
loc. cit
. shows that

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\to\iota_{*}^{r}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
corresponds to a map of
non-negatively graded modules that is an isomorphism in degree
0
,
so Lemma
7.3.2.1
shows that the maps is the above
limit are isomorphisms as well. Therefore, we may calculate this
limit as:

(7.3.3)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}}).

This term is:

\begin{gathered}\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{G}})=\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n},{\mathcal{G}})=\\
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})=\\
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})}(\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}).\end{gathered}

This last expression is another way of writing the left hand side
of Proposition
7.3.1.1
. It is straightforward to see that the isomorphism
we have just constructed is inverse to the map in
loc. cit
., yielding the result.

7.4.
Structure of the argument

We are now positioned to prove Proposition
7.1.1.1
.
We begin by outlining the argument.

7.4.1.

The starting point is the following elementary observation.

Lemma 7.4.1.1
.

Suppose
f:M\to N\in W_{2}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
is a morphism (in the derived category) of
modules over the Weyl algebra
W_{2}
in two variables,
with generators denoted
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\partial_{\alpha}
and
\partial_{\beta}
.
Then
f
is an isomorphism if and only if the morphisms:

(7.4.1)

\begin{gathered}\underset{r,\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muM\coloneqq\operatorname{colim}(M\xrightarrow{\alpha}M\xrightarrow{\alpha}\ldots)\to\underset{r,\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muN\\
\underset{r,\beta}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muM\to\underset{r,\beta}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muN\\
M^{\alpha}/\beta\to N^{\alpha}/\beta\end{gathered}

are isomorphisms in
\mathsf{Vect}
.
In the last line, we define e.g.
M^{\alpha}
as the
(homotopy) kernel of
\alpha:M\to M
