, and the quotient by
\beta
means
the homotopy cokernel of the induced map
\beta:M^{\alpha}\to M^{\alpha}

(which exists because
\alpha
and
\beta
commute).

Proof.

In terms of
D
-modules on
{\mathbb{A}}^{2}
, our assumptions are that
f
induces an isomorphism
on restriction to
({\mathbb{A}}^{1}\setminus 0)\times{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
,
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}\setminus 0)
,
and on
!
-restriction to
0
(up to a cohomological shift). This implies the claim by

D
-module excision.

∎

7.4.2.

We choose coordinates on the disc
{\mathcal{D}}
. We obtain an isomorphism

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{n}
; we let
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{n-1}
denote the coordinates
(so e.g.
\alpha_{0}=\operatorname{ev}
as maps to
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
).
We obtain an evident decomposition
W_{n}\simeq W_{1}\otimes W_{n-1}
, where the first
tensor factor uses the coordinate
\alpha_{0}
.

Let
f
denote the morphism
W_{n}^{op}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\in\mathsf{Alg}
.
As we have a morphism
W_{1}^{op}\to W_{n}^{op}
of algebras as above, we may regard
f

as a morphism of
W_{1}
-bimodules (via left and right multiplication).

Our argument applies Lemma
7.4.1.1
using this bimodule structure.
Generally speaking, the first two conditions from the lemma
amount to Proposition
7.3.1.1
and its relatives,
and the last one is given by induction.

7.5.
Verifying the axioms

7.5.1.

In the setting of §
7.4.2
,
it remains to verify the conditions of Lemma
7.4.1.1
.
We do so below.

7.5.2.
Colimits

We begin with checking the first two maps from (
7.4.1
) are isomorphisms.

Clearly:

\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muW_{n}=\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muW_{n}=W_{n}[\alpha_{0}^{-1}]=\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

I.e., in our example, the left hand sides of the first two equations in (
7.4.1
)
each identify with global differential operators on
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Below, we verify the same for the right hand sides in our examples. Then we check that our map (
f
, in the notation of Lemma
7.4.1.1
), is compatible
with these identifications.

7.5.3.

The left action of
\alpha_{0}
on
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})

is, by construction, obtained by right composition with the map:

{\mathcal{F}}_{n}=\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}(i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))\to\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}(\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}))={\mathcal{F}}_{n}

obtained by applying
\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}
to
the canonical map:

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}.

Therefore, by compactness of
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
, we see that:

\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}\underset{r}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu(\iota_{*}^{r,\mathsf{IndCoh}})i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

The right hand side clearly identifies with:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\zeta_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})).

Unwinding the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
, we can further identify it with:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}\big(i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\big).

The comparison of (
7.3.2
) and (
7.3.3
)
from §
7.3.6
yields that the map:

\begin{gathered}\Gamma\big(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\big)=\\
\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\big({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w})({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\big)=\\
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\big)\to\\
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n})}\big(i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}},i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\big)\end{gathered}

is an isomorphism.

By Theorem
6.3.1.1
, the left hand side identifies canonically with:

\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

Putting this together, we obtain an isomorphism:

\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

7.5.4.

In §
7.5.3
, we constructed an isomorphism:

\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}).

We claim that the resulting map:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\to\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

is in fact (canonically) a map of (DG) algebras, at least
once the target is given the opposite multiplication.

For convenience, define:

\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n}\coloneqq\zeta_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}.

Note that there is a canonical morphism:

{\mathcal{F}}_{n}\to\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n}.

We in effect showed that the natural maps:

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n})\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n})\leftarrow\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})}(\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})

are isomorphisms. The map:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\to\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n})

is clearly a map of right

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})
-modules.
But
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n})
carries
a commuting left

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(\mathring{{\mathcal{F}}}_{n})
-module structure,
for which it is a free module of rank
1
by the above.
As the class field theory isomorphism:

(7.5.1)

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n})}(\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

is an isomorphism of algebras, we obtain the claim.

7.5.5.

The above calculations verify that there are canonical isomorphisms:

\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muW_{n}\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})\simeq\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}).

We claim that this map is induced by our comparison map
f
from
§
7.4.2
.

By §
7.5.4
, it suffices to check that the composition:

W_{n}^{op}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\to\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\simeq\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})^{op}

is the restriction map. We first check this on some particular elements.

For the linear functions
t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt\subseteq W_{n}^{op}
,
this follows by construction. Indeed, the geometric incarnation of the
action of these elements is the observation that

{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
is closed under
the action of
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{pos,\leq n}
. The same is true
for
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
—
in fact, it is closed under
\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}
. Tracing the
constructions, these observations provide the claim.

Next, we claim that the vector fields on
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
defined by the
infinitesimal
{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action:

k[[t]]/t^{n}\simeq\operatorname{Lie}({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\subseteq W_{n}^{op}

have the correct image. Indeed, this follows by
construction of the class field theory isomorphism and Lemma
6.6.2.1
.

By the above, we see that the algebra map:

W_{n}^{op}\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})^{op}

factors through the (non-commutative) localization of

W_{n}^{op}
at
\alpha_{0}
, i.e., through a map:

W_{n}^{op}\to\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})^{op}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})^{op}

for some algebra map
\gamma
. As the subspaces

t^{-n}k[[t]]dt/k[[t]]dt
and
k[[t]]/t^{n}

generate
\Gamma({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})^{op}
as an algebra,
and as we have seen
\gamma
is the identity on these elements,
\gamma

itself must be the identity map, as desired.

7.5.6.

Next, we calculate:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}).

For
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, let
\prescript{r}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}

denote the closed where the meromorphic section
s
has a pole of order
\leq r
;
therefore,
\prescript{0}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}
,

\iota({\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n})=\prescript{-1}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
, and
\alpha_{0}
can be
considered as obtained by restriction

\prescript{r}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to\prescript{r-1}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}
for any
r
.

We let
i_{n,r}:\prescript{r}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n}
denote
the embedding.

For any
{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})
, it follows by definition
that:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n},{\mathcal{G}})=\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(i_{n,r,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\prescript{r}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}}),\operatorname{Oblv}{\mathcal{G}}).

We have a canonical isomorphism:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}(i_{n,r,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\prescript{r}{}{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\leq n}}),\operatorname{Oblv}{\mathcal{G}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}{\mathcal{G}}).

Now taking
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{F}}_{n}
, we see that:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\simeq\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

By the analysis of §
7.3.5
,
60
60

60

Specifically, the
observations that the ring
A_{n}
from

loc. cit
. is positively graded with degree
0
component corresponding to

\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}=\operatorname{Spec}(A_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
,
the map:

\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\to\Gamma^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\widetilde{\zeta}_{*}^{\mskip 3.0mu\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}})

is an isomorphism. By functoriality, the target identifies with:

\Gamma(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n},\operatorname{Oblv}\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n},w}{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m},\log}^{\leq n}}).

Now passing to the colimit over
\alpha_{0}
acting on the left and
right simultaneously, the above analysis actually shows that the maps:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\to\underset{(r_{1},r_{2}),-\cdot\alpha_{0},-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\leftarrow\underset{r,\alpha_{0}\cdot-}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}.

are isomorphisms. It then follows from §
7.5.5
that
the comparison map:

\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0muW_{n}\to\underset{r,-\cdot\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})

is an isomorphism.

7.5.7.
Hamiltonian reduction

It remains to check that the third map in (
7.4.1
)
is an isomorphism. We will see that this amounts to our
inductive hypothesis.

7.5.8.

We begin with some notation.

Suppose
A
is a (DG) algebra, and that we are given a map:

k[t]\to A.

We let
\alpha\in\Omega^{\infty}A
denote the image of
t
.

In this case, we let:

\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha}(A)\coloneqq A^{\alpha\cdot-}/-\cdot\alpha

with notation on the right hand side understood as
in Lemma
7.4.1.1
, i.e., we take the homotopy quotient of
right multiplication by
\alpha
and then the homotopy kernel of the
left action of
\alpha
(and of course, the order of these two operations is
not important).

As is standard, we refer to
\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha}(A)

as the
quantum Hamiltonian reduction

of
A
by
\alpha
.

7.5.9.

First, we note that
\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha_{0}}(W_{1}^{op})=k
(with
1\in k
mapping to the
unit in
W_{1}
), so similarly,

\operatorname{QHH}_{\alpha_{0}}(W_{n}^{op})=W_{n-1}^{op}
. This is the left hand side of
the third map in (
7.4.1
) in our setting.

7.5.10.

Next, we calculate the right hand side of (
7.4.1
).

First, we have:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}))

by Remark
7.1.1.2
(and the assumption that
n>0
).

By (
2.8.1
), we then have:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)^{\alpha_{0}\cdot-}=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}),\lambda_{n-1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n-1})\big).

Applying (
2.8.1
) again, we have:

(7.5.2)

\begin{gathered}\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)\Big)\coloneqq\\
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)^{\alpha_{0}\cdot-}/-\cdot\alpha_{0}=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n-1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n-1})\big).\end{gathered}

By induction, we know that the right hand side identifies with

W_{n-1}^{op}
as an algebra. Below, we will show that the
comparison map from Lemma
7.4.1.1
coincides with this
identification.

7.5.11.

Let us return to the general setting of §
7.5.8
.

First, observe that
\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha}(A)
carries a canonical algebra structure.
Indeed, it obviously identifies with:

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(A/-\cdot\alpha)=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(A\underset{k[t]}{\otimes}k).

(Here
k[t]
acts on
k
with
t
acting by
0
.)
We equip
A^{\alpha\cdot-}/-\cdot\alpha
with the multiplication

opposite
to this one.
61
61

61

This convention is natural
because
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(A)=A^{op}
.

Suppose now that
A
and
B
are (DG) algebras, and that we are given a map:

B[t]\coloneqq B\otimes k[t]\to A.

We can further identify:

A\underset{k[t]}{\otimes}k\simeq A\underset{B[t]}{\otimes}B.

I.e.,
A\underset{k[t]}{\otimes}k
is canonically
an
(A,B)
-bimodule. Therefore, by construction, we obtain
a canonical morphism:

B\to\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha}(A).

Remark 7.5.11.1
.

We note that the diagram:

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/-\cdot\alpha}

commutes by construction.

7.5.12.

We apply the above with:

A=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n})}({\mathcal{F}}_{n}),\alpha=\alpha_{0},B=W_{n-1}^{op}.

We first see that:

\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)\Big)

carries a canonical algebra structure; by construction,
(
7.5.2
) is an isomorphism of algebras, where the right hand side
is given the usual algebra structure on endomorphisms.

Moreover, we deduce that the map:

W_{n-1}^{op}\to\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)\Big).

(coming from Lemma
7.4.1.1
) is an algebra morphism.

7.5.13.

Finally, we claim that the resulting map:

W_{n-1}^{op}\to\operatorname{QH}_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n})\big)\Big)=\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}\big(\lambda_{n-1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{F}}_{n-1})\big)

coincides with the construction of §
5
(for
n-1
instead of
n
).

Indeed, as this is an algebra map, we can check this on generators of

W_{n-1}
. There it is essentially tautological from the constructions.

In particular, we see by induction that this map is an isomorphism.
Therefore, the conditions of Lemma
7.4.1.1
are satisfied,
so we have proved Proposition
7.1.1.1
.

8.
Proof of the main theorem

8.1.
Overview

In this section we prove Theorem
1.1.0.1
. The idea is simple given the
work we have done so far: bootstrap from the functors
\Delta_{n}
constructed
earlier.

8.2.
Arcs

We begin by constructing a strongly
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant functor:

\Delta_{\infty}:D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}).

8.2.1.
Notation

In what follows, we let:

p_{n}:{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

denote the structural map.
For
m\geq n
, we similarly let:

p_{n,m}:{\mathfrak{L}}_{m}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

denote the structural map.

8.2.2.
Overview

First, let us unwind what it means to construct such a functor

\Delta_{\infty}
.

Recall from
[
Ras3
]
that by definition we have:

D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\coloneqq\operatorname{colim}_{n\geq 0}\mskip 3.0muD({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})

where the structural functors are the standard

!
-pullback functors of
D
-module theory. We let:

p_{n}^{!}:D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})

denote the structural functor.

The above colimit is a colimit of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories.
Moreover, as it is indexed by
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
, it suffices to
construct functors:

(8.2.1)

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\in D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}

and commutative diagrams:

(8.2.2)

\textstyle{D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{n,n+1}^{!}}
\textstyle{D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}

of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories for each
n
.
(Here
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
acts on
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
via
geometric class field theory, as in §
6
.)

8.2.3.

For us, by definition, the functor (
8.2.1
)
is
\Delta_{n}
. It remains to construct the commutative
diagrams (
8.2.2
) as above.

In §
8.2.4
-
8.2.5
, we will formulate
a compatibility for these commutative diagrams that
characterizes them uniquely. We then turn to proving their existence.

8.2.4.
Preliminary constructions with differential operators

We digress to some general constructions with
D
-modules, continuing
the discussion from §
6.1
.

Suppose
\pi:X\to Y
is a morphism between smooth varieties. In this
case, one has a canonical natural isomorphism:

\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}\pi^{!}\simeq\pi^{*}\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}

of functors:

D(Y)\to\mathsf{QCoh}(X).

Here
\pi^{*}
is the quasi-coherent pullback functor. We refer
to
[
GR1
]
for the construction.

The canonical map:

{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\to\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}\operatorname{ind}^{\ell}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})=\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}(D_{Y})

then gives rise to a map:

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}(D_{Y})\simeq\operatorname{Oblv}^{\ell}\pi^{!}(D_{Y})

so by adjunction a map:

(8.2.3)

\operatorname{can}:D_{X}\to\pi^{!}(D_{Y}).

In the special case where
\pi
is smooth, the map
\operatorname{can}

is between objects concentrated in cohomological degree
-\dim X
, and
is an epimorphism in that abelian category.

Finally, we note that if an algebraic group
G
acts on
X
and
Y
, and the map

\pi
is
G
-equivariant, the map
\operatorname{can}
upgrades canonically to a map
of
G
-weakly equivariant
D
-modules (by functoriality of the above constructions).

8.2.5.
A compatibility

Let us now return to our setting. For each
n
, the above
provides canonical morphisms:

\operatorname{can}_{n}:D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\to p_{n,n+1}^{!}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\in D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}.

Suppose we are given a commutative diagram (
8.2.2
).
We then obtain a morphism:

(8.2.4)

\begin{gathered}i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})\eqqcolon\Delta_{n+1}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\Delta_{n+1}(\operatorname{can}_{n})}}\Delta_{n+1}(p_{n,n+1}^{!}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\overset{\eqref{eq:delta-infty-comm}}{\simeq}\\
\Delta_{n}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\coloneqq i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w}\simeq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}).\end{gathered}

(We have omitted terms
\lambda_{-,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}
to simplify
the notation. We are also abusing notation in letting
e.g.
\Delta_{n}
denote the induced functor on
weakly equivariant categories.)

On the other hand, there is another such map coming from the embedding

\delta_{n+1}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}
and the adjunction map:

(8.2.5)

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}\to\delta_{n+1,*}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}).

We will show:

Proposition 8.2.5.1
.

There exist unique commutative diagrams (
8.2.2
)
so that the resulting map (
8.2.4
) is (
8.2.5
).

In this remainder of this subsection, we prove Proposition
8.2.5.1
.

8.2.6.

Let us make a preliminary observation in a general setting. Fix
G

an affine algebraic group.

Suppose that
A
is an associative algebra with a

G
-action and a Harish-Chandra datum as in §
6.5
, so

G
acts strongly on
A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
.

Suppose we are given
{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}}\in G\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}
and functors:

\begin{gathered}F_{1}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to{\mathcal{C}}\\
F_{2}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}\\
F_{3}:A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\to{\mathcal{D}}.\end{gathered}

We let:

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\coloneqq F_{1}(A)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w},{\mathcal{F}}_{3}\coloneqq F_{3}(A)\in{\mathcal{D}}^{G,w}.

Moreover, to remove discussion of higher homotopical considerations,
we assume:

•

A
is classical.

•

\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})
and
\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}({\mathcal{F}}_{3})
are classical.

Then, as in §
6.5
, the
functor
F_{1}
is completely encoded in the datum of compact
object
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{G,w}
with its action of
A^{op}\in\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Rep}(G))
by
endomorphisms (the data should satisfy the property that the action of

{\mathfrak{g}}
through
i:{\mathfrak{g}}\to A
coincides with the obstruction to strong equivariance).
The same applies for
F_{3}
.

Therefore, we see that (under the homotopically simplifying assumptions
above), the data of a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{A\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{F_{3}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}}

is equivalent to giving an isomorphism:

F_{2}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{3}\in{\mathcal{D}}^{G,w}

compatible with the actions of
A^{op}\in\mathsf{Rep}(G)
on both sides.
(In particular, under these assumptions, it is equivalent to construct
a commutative diagram of strongly or weakly
G
-equivariant categories.)

8.2.7.

We apply this in our setting as follows.

First, the actions on the source categories factor through the localization

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\to D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
, and the functors
factor through the subcategory
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq n+1})\subseteq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
,
through which
D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
acts. So the question only concerns the
action of an affine algebraic group, not an affine group scheme.

We then see
it suffices to show that there is a unique isomorphism:

(8.2.6)

\Delta_{n+1}(p_{n,n+1}^{!}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq\Delta_{n}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\coloneqq i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})

such that:

•

The two resulting maps from
i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})

(one being (
8.2.4
), the other being the canonical map)
coincide.

•

The two resulting actions of
W_{n}^{op}
on

\operatorname{Av}_{!}^{\operatorname{Gr}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq n}}i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})
(coming from
the tautological action of

W_{n}^{op}
on
D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\in D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
, and functoriality of

\Delta_{n+1}p_{n,n+1}^{!}
and
\Delta_{n}
respectively) coincide
under the isomorphism.

As the canonical map:

i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})\to i_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}})\in\mathsf{Coh}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log})^{\heartsuit}

is an epimorphism, we see that the first point above
implies that such an isomorphism is unique if it exists.

To see existence, it is convenient to fix a coordinate
t
on the
disc. We use the
notation
\alpha
notation of §
7.4.2
.

We have an evident short exact sequence:

0\to D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}(-1)\xrightarrow{-\cdot\partial\alpha_{n}}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}_{n}}p_{n,n+1}^{!}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\to 0

in the abelian category

D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w,\heartsuit}[n+1]
.
Here in the first term, the twist
(-1)
indicates that we modify the
weakly equivariant structure by tensoring with the representation

k(-1)\in\mathsf{Rep}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
obtained by restriction along
\operatorname{ev}
from the
standard representation of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

By construction of
\Delta_{n+1}
, on weakly equivariant
categories, we have:

\begin{gathered}\Delta_{n+1}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})\\
\Delta_{n+1}(D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}(-1))\simeq i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}).\end{gathered}

Moreover, it is easy to see that
\Delta_{n+1}(-\cdot\partial_{\alpha_{n}})

goes to the map induced from:

\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})\xrightarrow{b_{-n}\cdot-}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}},

i.e., the left arrow in (
2.8.3
). From
(
2.8.3
), it follows that there is a unique
isomorphism (
8.2.6
) compatible with the projection from

i_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})
. It is straightforward
to check that this isomorphism is compatible with the action of

W_{n}^{op}
, concluding the argument.

8.3.
Compatibility with translation

We now establish an additional equivariance property
for the functor
\Delta_{\infty}
.

Specifically, recall the positive loop space
{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subseteq{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

for
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, as defined in §
3.5
.
This space is an ind-closed submonoid of
{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
containing

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, so we have
a fully-faithful monoidal functor:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\subseteq D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}).

By construction,
{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts on
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, so

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
acts on
D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
.

On the other hand, we have the local class field theory isomorphism:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

defining a (fully faithful, symmetric) monoidal functor:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\to\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})

and so an action of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
on

\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Our goal is to canonically upgrade the strong

{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariance of
\Delta_{\infty}
to make
\Delta_{\infty}

into a morphism of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories.

8.3.1.

Before proceeding, we wish to pin down this equivariance property uniquely.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}
is a
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module category.
We obtain a canonical functor
T:{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}

given by the action of (the
\delta

D
-module at)
some (equivalently any) uniformizer
t\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Our uniqueness property will involve the corresponding functors
T
on both
sides. Below, we collect preliminary observations regarding how the
functor
T
interacts with the source and target of our functor.

8.3.2.

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}=D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
. A choice of uniformizer
t
defines
a closed embedding
\mu_{t}:{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
given by multiplication
by
t
. The corresponding functor
T
above is given by the left adjoint
to the pullback functor:

\mu_{t}^{!}:D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}).

I.e., in the notation of
[
Ras3
]
, we have
T=\mu_{t,*,!-dR}
.

By adjunction, we observe that there is a canonical map:

(8.3.1)

\operatorname{can}:T(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})=\mu_{t,*,!-dR}(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})=\mu_{t,*,!-dR}\mu_{t}^{!}(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\to\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}.

8.3.3.

Now suppose
{\mathcal{C}}=\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
. Then we have:

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})^{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\simeq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\simeq\\
\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq 0})\underset{\mathsf{QCoh}(\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}})}{\otimes}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}{\times}\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\leq 0})=\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 0}).\end{gathered}

Under the above
identifications, the functor
T
is given by tensoring with
the
dual
62
62

62

Because the Contou-Carrère pairing
is skew symmetric, one has to fix a convention in setting up geometric
class field theory. The sign compatibility was implicitly fixed in
§
3
. It is a straightforward check to trace the constructions
to see that our sign conventions force the appearance of the dual to the
standard representation; essentially, we are on the “dual” side of the
Contou-Carrère duality.

to the standard representation of
{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, i.e., tensoring with the
bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\leq 0}}(1)
considered earlier.

Now observe that there is a canonical map:

(8.3.2)

\operatorname{can}:T({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}})={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}(1)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}

from (
2.8.2
).

8.3.4.

We can now formulate our results precisely.

Observe that by construction, we have
an isomorphism:

\Delta_{\infty}(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})=\Delta_{0}(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{0}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq i_{0,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 0}).

(Here we consider
\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
as a
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant
object.)

Proposition 8.3.4.1
.

There is a unique structure of

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-equivariance on the functor
\Delta_{\infty}

such that:

•

The underlying
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-equivariance is the one
constructed in §
8.2
.

•

The map:

T({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}})\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq 0}}\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq 0})

obtained by functoriality from:

\Delta_{\infty}(\operatorname{can}:T(\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:can-unit-geom}}\omega_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})

coincides with (
8.3.2
).

Proof.

We begin with the existence. For this, it is convenient to fix a
uniformizer
t
once and for all. Note that this choice
induces a morphism
\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
using
Proposition
2.3.5.1
. We let
{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LocSys}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}(-1)
and

{\mathcal{Y}}(-1)
denote
the corresponding line bundles, and generally use notation as in
§
2.7.2
.

We obtain a splitting:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\otimes D({\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0})

of (symmetric) monoidal DG categories. Therefore, we find
that
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories are equivalent to

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories with an endofunctor.
Therefore, the existence amounts to showing that
\Delta_{\infty}

intertwines
\mu_{t,*,!-dR}
with tensoring with

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(1)
.

Unwinding the constructions, this amounts to constructing commutative diagrams
of strong
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-module categories:

\textstyle{D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{t,*,dR}}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{n}}
\textstyle{D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(1)\otimes-}
\textstyle{\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})}

compatibly in
n
(using the
p_{-}^{!}
functors and
the commutativity data of Proposition
8.2.5.1
);
here we have also let
\mu_{t}
denote the induced map

{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
given by multiplication with
t
.

Using the logic of (
8.2.6
) (as in
the proof of Proposition
8.2.5.1
),
we see that this amounts to constructing
isomorphisms:

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(1)\simeq\Delta_{n+1}(\mu_{t,*,dR}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\in\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}_{\log}^{\leq n+1})

satisfying some compatibilities (that do not involve higher
homotopy theory).

For this, we observe that there is a standard short exact sequence:

0\to D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\xrightarrow{-\cdot\alpha_{0}}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}(1)\xrightarrow{}\mu_{t,*,dR}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}[1]\to 0

in the abelian category
D({\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n+1}{\mathbb{G}}_{m},w,\heartsuit}[n+1]

(with notation as before).
The map
-\cdot\alpha_{0}
maps under
\Delta_{n+1}
to the canonical
map:

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}\to\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}).

Therefore, we obtain an isomorphism:

\Delta_{n+1}(\mu_{t,*,dR}D_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{n}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})\simeq\operatorname{Coker}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}[-1]\to\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}[-1]))\simeq\operatorname{Ker}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}}\to\iota_{*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n+1}})).

The latter is canonically isomorphism to

\delta_{n+1,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(1)
by (
2.8.1
),
as desired.

This isomorphism is easily seen to be compatible with the actions of
W_{n}^{op}

and with varying
n
, proving existence.

For uniqueness, we observe that
\Delta_{\infty}
is fully faithful
(cf. Lemma
8.4.0.2
below), so the observation follows from the
observation that:

k\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{End}_{\mathsf{End}_{{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\text{\textendash}\mathsf{mod}}(D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}))}(\mu_{t,!,dR})\in\mathsf{Gpd}

(which e.g. follows by replacing functors with kernels
on the square
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
),
meaning that any isomorphism we construct is unique up to scalars;
clearly the second compatibility in the proposition pins down this
scalar multiple uniquely.

∎

8.4.
Conclusion of the argument

We now have the following precise form of our main theorem.

Theorem 8.4.0.1
.

There is a unique equivalence:

\Delta:D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})

of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories such that the induced morphism:

D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\subseteq D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})

of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories coincides with the functor

\Delta_{\infty}
from above.

Proof.

There is a natural functor:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}{\otimes}D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\to D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})

that we observe is an equivalence. Indeed, this follows
by choosing a coordinate
t
on the disc as before, splitting

{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
as
{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{Z}}
, which then implies:

D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}{\otimes}D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\simeq\operatorname{colim}\mskip 3.0mu\big(D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\xrightarrow{\mu_{t,*,!-dR}}D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\xrightarrow{\mu_{t,*,!-dR}}\ldots\big).

Using
t^{-n}
to identify the
n
th term here with

t^{-n}\cdot{\mathfrak{L}}^{+}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\subseteq{\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and applying the
definition from
[
Ras3
]
, we see that this
colimit canonically identifies with
D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
compatibly with the
natural functor considered above.

Therefore, there is a unique functor:

\Delta:D^{!}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})

of
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-module categories restricting to

\Delta_{\infty}
(compatibly with the
D^{*}({\mathfrak{L}}^{pos}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
-equivariance).
It remains to show that
\Delta
is an equivalence.

First, we note that
\Delta_{\infty}
is fully faithful by
Proposition
7.1.1.1
and Lemma
8.4.0.2
below. Next,
we deduce that
\Delta
itself is fully faithful by another
application of Lemma
8.4.0.2
.

Therefore, it remains to show that
\Delta
is essentially surjective.
Clearly its essential image contains each object:

\pi_{n,*}^{\mathsf{IndCoh}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\leq n}}(m))

for all
n\geq 0
,
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Therefore, it suffices
to show that
\mathsf{IndCoh}^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
is generated under colimits by these objects.
But this follows immediately from the definitions and Corollary
4.4.3.1
.

∎

Above, we used the following simple categorical lemma.

Lemma 8.4.0.2
.

Suppose we are given a filtered diagram
i\mapsto{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
of compactly
generated DG categories, and a functor:

F:{\mathcal{C}}\coloneqq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\mskip 3.0mu{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathsf{DGCat}_{cont}

preserving compact objects. Suppose each structural functor:

{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{j}

and each composition:

{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}

is fully faithful. Then
F
is fully faithful.
